. The proofs of these lemmas are classical bounds of martingale relying on some assumptions on the moments of the gradients. Notice here that
C
is no longer a constant and extra care of the analysis is necessary for the proof of Lemma
[
ZPVB23
, Lemma B.4]
. To use the lemmas
[
ZPVB23
, Lemmas B.2,B.3,B.4]
, we need to prove the bounds on the growth of gradients norms of
[
ZPVB23
, Lemma B.8]
. We check this

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{(y,r,{\bm{z}})}[\|\nabla_{\bm{w}}^{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}L({\bm{w}}_{t};{\bm{z}}_{t},r_{t},y_{t})\|^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{(y,r,{\bm{z}})}[\|{\mathsf{P}}_{{\bm{w}}_{t}}({\bm{z}}_{t})(Q_{\ell}^{(d)})^{\prime}(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}_{t}\rangle){\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y,r)\|^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{(y,r,{\bm{z}})}\left[\left|(Q_{\ell}^{(d)})^{\prime}(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}_{t}\rangle){\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y,r)\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C(d)^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|Q_{\ell-1}^{(d-2)}(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}\rangle)\right|^{2}{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y,r)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq d\ell\left(\frac{4e}{\ell}\right)^{\ell/2}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y,r)^{2}\right]\log(1/\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y,r)\right])^{\ell/2}

\displaystyle\leq Kd\ell\left(\frac{4e}{\ell}\right)^{\ell/2}\log(\|{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}\|_{4}^{2})^{\ell/2}

\displaystyle\leq d\mathcal{K}_{1}.

where we have used
Lemma

24
, the identity and the hypercontractivity and Jensen inequality in the last line.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{(y,r,z)}[\|\nabla_{\bm{w}}^{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}L({\bm{w}}_{t};{\bm{z}}_{t},r_{t},y_{t})\|^{4}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{(y,r,z)}[\|{\mathsf{P}}_{{\bm{w}}_{t}}({\bm{z}}_{t})(Q_{\ell}^{(d)})^{\prime}(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}_{t}\rangle){\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y,r)\|^{4}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{(y,r,{\bm{z}})}\left[\left|(Q_{\ell}^{(d)})^{\prime}(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}_{t}\rangle){\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y,r)\right|^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C(d)^{4}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|Q_{\ell-1}^{(d-2)}(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}\rangle)\right|^{4}\left|{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y,r)\right|^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 4C(d)^{4}\ell\left(\frac{4e}{4\ell}\right)^{4\ell/2}\|{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}\|_{4}^{4}\log\left(\frac{\|{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}\|_{8}^{2}}{\|{\mathcal{T}}\|_{4}^{4}}\right)^{4\ell/2}.

\displaystyle\leq 4d^{2}K\ell\left(\frac{4e}{4\ell}\right)^{4\ell/2}\|{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}\|_{4}^{4}\log\left(\frac{\|{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}\|_{8}^{2}}{\|{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}\|_{4}^{4}}\right)^{4\ell/2}

\displaystyle\leq d^{2}\mathcal{K}_{2}.

We have
{\bm{M}}({\bm{w}}_{t};{\bm{z}}_{t},r_{t},y_{t})=L({\bm{w}}_{t};{\bm{z}}_{t},r_{t},y_{t})-\mathbb{E}[L({\bm{w}}_{t};{\bm{z}}_{t},r_{t},y_{t})]
, hence

\displaystyle\langle\nabla^{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}_{{\bm{w}}}{\bm{M}}({\bm{w}}_{t};{\bm{z}}_{t},r_{t},y_{t}),{\bm{w}}_{*}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\nabla_{\bm{w}}^{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}(L({\bm{w}}_{t};{\bm{z}}_{t},r_{t},y_{t}),{\bm{w}}_{*}\rangle-\mathbb{E}[\langle\nabla_{\bm{w}}^{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}(L({\bm{w}}_{t};{\bm{z}}_{t},r_{t},y_{t}),{\bm{w}}^{*}\rangle]

\displaystyle-\langle{\bm{w}},\nabla_{\bm{w}}^{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}(L({\bm{w}}_{t};{\bm{z}}_{t},r_{t},y_{t})\rangle m+\mathbb{E}[m\langle{\bm{w}},\nabla_{\bm{w}}^{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}(L({\bm{w}}_{t};{\bm{z}}_{t},r_{t},y_{t})\rangle].

We then have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{(y,r,{\bm{z}})}[\langle\nabla_{\bm{w}}^{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}({\bm{M}}({\bm{w}}_{t};{\bm{z}}_{t},r_{t},y_{t}),{\bm{w}}_{*}\rangle^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq 2{\rm Var}_{(y,r,{\bm{z}})}(\nabla_{\bm{w}}^{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}(L({\bm{w}}_{t};{\bm{z}}_{t},r_{t},y_{t}),{\bm{w}}_{*}\rangle)+2{\rm Var}_{(y,r,{\bm{z}})}(\langle{\bm{w}},\nabla_{\bm{w}}^{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}(L({\bm{w}}_{t};{\bm{z}}_{t},r_{t},y_{t})\rangle m)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{z}}_{t},{\bm{w}}_{*}\rangle^{2}(Q_{\ell}^{(d)})^{\prime}(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}_{t}\rangle)^{2}{\mathcal{T}}(y,r)^{2}]+2\mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{z}}_{t},{\bm{w}}_{*}\rangle^{2}(Q_{\ell}^{(d)})^{\prime}(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}_{t}\rangle)^{2}{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y,r)^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq 2K\left(\frac{4e}{\ell}\right)^{\ell/2}\log(\|{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}\|_{4}^{2})^{\ell/2}:=\mathcal{K}_{3},

where we have used the inequality
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2(a^{2}+b^{2})
, and hypercontractivity of Gegenbauer polynomials (
lemma

20
).

Conditioned on the event
\{T\leq\tau^{-}_{2s^{*}/\sqrt{d}}\}
, and using the inequality (
68
), we have

m_{t+1}\geq\frac{1}{r_{t}}\left(m_{t}+2\eta(1-m_{t}^{2})\beta_{\ell,d}\frac{\ell\cdot(\ell+d-2)}{d-1}m_{t}^{\ell-1}-\eta\langle\nabla^{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}{\bm{M}}_{t},{\bm{w}}^{*}\rangle\right).

Using the following bound on
r_{t}
, which is for all
t\in{\mathbb{N}},
we have

1/r_{t}\geq 1-\eta^{2}\|\nabla_{{\bm{w}}_{t}}L({\bm{w}}_{t};y_{t},r_{t},{\bm{z}}_{t})\|^{2},

and plugging this into previous inequality, we have

m_{t+1}\geq m_{t}+2\eta(1-m_{t}^{2})\beta_{\ell,d}\frac{\ell\cdot(\ell+d-2)}{d-1}m_{t}^{\ell-1}-\eta\langle\nabla^{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}{\bm{M}}_{t},{\bm{w}}^{*}\rangle-\eta^{2}\left|m_{t}\right|\|\nabla_{{\bm{w}}_{t}}L({\bm{w}}_{t};y_{t},r_{t},{\bm{z}}_{t})\|^{2}-\eta^{3}\xi_{T},

(70)

where
\xi_{T}=\|\nabla_{{\bm{w}}}L({\bm{w}}_{T};y_{T},r_{T},{\bm{z}}_{T})\|^{2}\cdot|\langle\nabla_{{\bm{w}}}L({\bm{w}}_{T};y_{T},r_{T},{\bm{z}}_{T}),{\bm{w}}^{*}\rangle|^{2}.

We use
[
ZPVB23
, Lemma B.3]
to state that with probability at least
1-\frac{\mathcal{K}_{3}t\eta^{2}}{\lambda^{2}},
we have for all
\lambda>0
,

\sup_{t\leq T}\eta\left|\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\left\langle\nabla^{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}{\bm{M}}_{t},{\bm{w}}^{*}\right\rangle\right|\leq\lambda.

(71)

We employ
[
ZPVB23
, Lemma B.6]
to state that for all
\lambda>0
, with probability at least
1-\frac{\sqrt{\mathcal{K}_{1}\mathcal{K}_{2}}Td\eta^{3}}{\lambda},
we have

\sup_{t\leq T}\eta^{3}\sum_{k=0}^{t-1}\xi_{k}\leq\lambda.

(72)

We sum the equation (
E
) to obtain

\displaystyle m_{T}

\displaystyle\geq m_{0}+2\eta\frac{\beta_{\ell,d}\ell\cdot(\ell+d-2)}{d-1}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}(1-m_{t}^{2})m_{t}^{\ell-1}-\eta\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\langle\nabla^{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}{\bm{M}}_{t},{\bm{w}}^{*}\rangle

\displaystyle-\eta^{2}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left|m_{t}\right|\|\nabla_{{\bm{w}}_{t}}L({\bm{w}}_{t};y_{t},r_{t},{\bm{z}}_{t})\|^{2}-\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\eta^{3}\xi_{T},

We then use (
71
),(
72
) and plug it into (
E
), and use
\lambda=b/\sqrt{d}
, to state that with probability at least
1-\frac{\mathcal{K}_{3}Td^{1/2}\eta^{3}}{b^{2}}-\frac{\sqrt{\mathcal{K}_{1}\mathcal{K}_{2}}T\eta^{2}d^{-1}}{b}
, we have

m_{T}\geq\frac{7m_{0}}{10}+2\eta\frac{\beta_{\ell,d}\ell\cdot(\ell+d-2)}{d-1}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}(1-m_{t}^{2})m_{t}^{\ell-1}-\eta^{2}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left|m_{t}\right|\|\nabla_{{\bm{w}}_{t}}L({\bm{w}}_{t};y_{t},r_{t},{\bm{z}}_{t})\|^{2}.

(73)

We now bound the term coming from the projection step in inequality (
73
). We adapt the proof to take account the dependency on
\|{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}\|_{2}.

Lemma 11
.

For all
\lambda>0,
if for all
t\leq T,m_{t}\in[2b/\sqrt{d},1/2]
, and

\eta\leq\frac{c\|\xi_{d,\ell}\|_{2}}{d},
with probability at least
1-\frac{\mathcal{K}_{2}Td^{1/2}\eta^{2}}{\lambda}-\exp\left(-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2(\beta_{\ell,d}^{2}+\mathcal{K}_{2}d^{2}\eta^{2})\eta^{2}T+2\lambda\eta(\beta_{\ell,d}+\eta d^{3/2})}\right),
we have

\eta^{2}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left|m_{t}\right|\|\nabla_{{\bm{w}}_{t}}L({\bm{w}}_{t};y_{t},r_{t},{\bm{z}}_{t})\|^{2}+\eta\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}(1-m_{t}^{2})\beta_{d,\ell}\frac{\ell\cdot(\ell+d-2)}{d-1}m_{t}^{\ell-1}\leq 2\lambda.

Proof.

The proof is a slight adaptation of
[
ZPVB23
, Lemma B.4,B.5]
. An adaptation of the proof of Lemma B.4 gives us the following. For all
\lambda>0,
if for all
t\leq T,m_{t}\in[2b/\sqrt{d},1/2]
, and
\eta>0
, we have

\mathbb{P}(\eta\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}D_{t}\leq-\lambda)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2(\beta_{\ell,d}^{2}+\mathcal{K}_{2}d^{2}\eta^{2})\eta^{2}T+2\lambda\eta(\beta_{\ell,d}+\eta d^{3/2})}\right).

Besides, the adaptation of Lemma B.5 gives us

\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{t\leq T}\eta^{2}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}|m_{t}|\|\nabla_{\bm{w}}L\|^{2}1_{\|\nabla_{\bm{w}}L\|>d^{3/2}}\geq\lambda\right)\leq\frac{\mathcal{K}_{2}Td^{1/2}\eta^{2}}{\lambda}.

Combining the two inequalities, we end up the desired claim.
∎

We then use
\lambda=b/\sqrt{d},
and we obtain that with probability at least
1-p_{\eta,\mathcal{K}_{1},\mathcal{K}_{2},\mathcal{K}_{3}}
where

\displaystyle p_{\eta,\mathcal{K}_{1},\mathcal{K}_{2},\mathcal{K}_{3}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathcal{K}_{2}dT\eta^{2}}{b}+\exp\left(-\frac{b^{2}}{2(\beta_{k,d}^{2}+\mathcal{K}_{2}d^{2}\eta^{2})\eta^{2}dT+2bd^{1/2}\eta(\beta_{k,d}+\eta d^{3/2})}\right)+\frac{\mathcal{K}_{3}Td^{1/2}\eta^{3}}{b^{2}}-\frac{\sqrt{\mathcal{K}_{1}\mathcal{K}_{2}}T\eta^{2}d^{-1}}{b},

we have

m_{T}\geq\frac{m_{0}}{2}+\beta_{\ell,d}\frac{\ell\cdot(\ell+d-2)}{d-1}\eta\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}(1-m_{t}^{2})m_{t}^{\ell-1}.

Conditioned on the event
\{T\leq\tau_{1/2}^{+}\wedge\tau^{-}_{2s^{*}/\sqrt{d}}\},
we have the following inequality

m_{T}\geq\frac{s^{*}}{\sqrt{d}}+\eta\beta_{\ell,d}\frac{\ell\cdot(\ell+d-2)}{(d-1)2^{\ell+1}}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}m_{t}^{\ell-1}.

∎

Appendix F
Harmonic tensor unfolding

In this appendix, we analyze the harmonic tensor unfolding estimators (

TU

-

Alg

-

b

) and (

TU

-

Alg

), with integer
\ell\geq 3
, and prove the guarantees in Theorem
4
. For simplicity, we assume throughout that the transformation
{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}:\mathcal{Y}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\to\mathbb{R}
is bounded, with

\|{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}\|_{L^{2}}=1,\quad\|{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}\leq\kappa_{\ell},\qquad\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{d}}[{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(Y,R)Q_{\ell}(Z)]=\beta_{d,\ell}.

(74)

Without loss of generality, we take
\beta_{d,\ell}>0
. We describe in Remark
F.1
how to relax this condition.

F.1
Algorithms and guarantees

Consider first the naive tensor unfolding algorithm. Compute the empirical tensor

\hat{{\bm{T}}}:=\frac{1}{\mathsf{m}}\sum_{i\in[\mathsf{m}]}{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y_{i},r_{i}){\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}_{i})\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\otimes\ell},

where
{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})
is the degree-
\ell
harmonic tensor (see Section
F.3
).
Consider the ‘unfolded’ matrix

\textbf{Mat}_{I,J}(\hat{\bm{T}})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d^{I}\times{d^{J}}},\quad\text{with $I=J=\ell/2$ if $\ell$ even, and $I=\left\lfloor\ell/2\right\rfloor$, $J=\left\lfloor\ell/2\right\rfloor+1$ o.w.},

and compute

{\bm{s}}_{1}(\textbf{Mat}_{I,J}(\hat{\bm{T}}))\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d^{\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor}},

the top left singular vector of
\textbf{Mat}_{I,J}(\hat{\bm{T}})
. We then estimate
\hat{\bm{w}}
via

\hat{\bm{w}}:=\textbf{Vec}\left({\bm{s}}_{1}\left(\textbf{Mat}_{I,J}(\hat{\bm{T}})\right)\right),

(
\mathsf{TU}
-
\mathsf{Alg}
-
\mathsf{b}
)

where the mapping
\textbf{Vec}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d^{k}}\rightarrow\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
applied to
{\bm{u}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d^{k}}
returns the top left eigenvector of the folded matrix
\textbf{Mat}_{1,k-1}({\bm{u}})\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d^{k-1}}
, that is,

\textbf{Vec}({\bm{u}})=\argmax_{{\bm{w}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\bm{w}}^{\mathsf{T}}[\textbf{Mat}_{1,k-1}({\bm{u}})\textbf{Mat}_{1,k-1}({\bm{u}})^{\mathsf{T}}]{\bm{w}}.

We first show the following guarantee.

Theorem 8

(Balanced Harmonic tensor unfolding,
\ell
even)
.

Let
\nu_{d}\in\mathfrak{L}_{d}
be a spherical SIM and
\ell
be an even integer. Consider
{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}
a transformation satisfying (
74
). Their exists a universal constant
C_{\ell}>0
such that the following holds. For
\delta>0
, given
m
samples
(y_{i},{\bm{x}}_{i})\sim_{iid}\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}_{*}}
with

m\geq C_{\ell}\kappa_{\ell}\frac{d^{\ell/2}}{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}}\left[1+\beta_{d,\ell}\log(d/\delta)\right],

(75)

the estimator
\hat{\bm{w}}
in (

TU

-

Alg

-

b

) satisfies
|\langle\hat{\bm{w}},{\bm{w}}_{*}\rangle|\geq 1/4
with probability at least
1-\delta
. Furthermore,
\hat{\bm{w}}
can be computed with power iteration in
O_{d}(md^{\ell/2}\log(d))
runtime.

We prove the sample guarantee of Theorem
8
in Section
F.4
and the runtime guarantee in Section
F.6
. Thus, when
\ell
is even and choosing
{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}
such that
\beta_{d,\ell}=\Theta(\|\xi_{d,\ell}\|_{L^{2}})
, the algorithm (

TU

-

Alg

-

b

) achieves almost optimal sample and runtime on
V_{d,\ell}
:

\mathsf{m}\asymp\frac{d^{\ell/2}}{\|\xi_{d,\ell}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}\log(d),\qquad{\mathsf{T}}\asymp\frac{d^{\ell}}{\|\xi_{d,\ell}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}\log^{2}(d).

When
\|\xi_{d,\ell}\|_{L^{2}}=O(1/\log(d))
, then this algorithm achieves optimal sample complexity
\mathsf{m}\asymp d^{\ell/2}/\|\xi_{d,\ell}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
. When
\ell
is odd, however, the algorithm (

TU

-

Alg

-

b

) requires
\mathsf{m}\asymp d^{\lceil\ell/2\rceil}/\|\xi_{d,\ell}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
and is suboptimal by a factor
d^{1/2}
. This is due to the covariance structure of the Harmonic tensor
{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})
: a similar problem, with same suboptimality, arises for tensor PCA with symmetric noise
[
MR14
]
(if the noise is not symmetric and all entries are independent, then optimal complexity is achieved by the naive tensor unfolding algorithm
[
HSS15
]
).

Here, we modify (

TU

-

Alg

-

b

) by removing the diagonal elements. Consider integers
a,b\geq 1
such that
a<b
and
a+b=\ell
. Introduce the matrices

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{M}}_{1}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i\in[m]}{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y_{i},r_{i})\textbf{Mat}_{a,b}({\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}_{i}))\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{a}\times d^{b}},

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{M}}_{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m^{2}}\sum_{i\in[m]}{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y_{i},r_{i})^{2}\textbf{Mat}_{a,b}({\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}_{i}))\textbf{Mat}_{b,a}({\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}_{i}))\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{a}\times d^{a}},

and

\hat{\bm{M}}=\hat{\bm{M}}_{1}\hat{\bm{M}}_{1}^{\mathsf{T}}-\hat{\bm{M}}_{2}=\frac{1}{m^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y_{i},r_{i}){\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y_{j},r_{j})\textbf{Mat}_{a,b}({\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}_{i}))\textbf{Mat}_{b,a}({\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}_{j}))\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{a}\times d^{a}}.

Note that

\mathbb{E}[{\bm{M}}]=(1-m^{-1})\mathbb{E}[{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y,r)\textbf{Mat}_{a,b}({\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}))]\mathbb{E}[{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y,r)\textbf{Mat}_{a,b}({\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}))]^{\mathsf{T}}\approx[{\bm{w}}_{*}^{\otimes a}][{\bm{w}}_{*}^{\otimes a}]^{\mathsf{T}}.

Thus, we define our tensor unfolding estimator to be

\hat{\bm{w}}:=\textbf{Vec}\left({\bm{s}}_{1}\left(\hat{\bm{M}}\right)\right),

(
\mathsf{TU}
-
\mathsf{Alg}
)

where
{\bm{s}}_{1}(\hat{\bm{M}})
is the top left eigenvector of
\hat{\bm{M}}
.

Theorem 9

(Harmonic tensor unfolding)
.

Let
\nu_{d}\in\mathfrak{L}_{d}
be a spherical SIM and let
a,b\geq 1
be two integers such that
a<b
and
a+b=\ell
. Consider
{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}
a transformation satisfying (
74
). There exist universal constants
c_{\ell},C_{\ell}>0
that only depend on
\ell
such that the following holds. Given
m
samples
(y_{i},{\bm{x}}_{i})\sim_{iid}\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}_{*}}
with

m\geq C_{\ell}\kappa_{\ell}^{2}\frac{d^{\ell/2}}{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}},

(76)

the estimator
\hat{\bm{w}}
in (

TU

-

Alg

) satisfies
|\langle\hat{\bm{w}},{\bm{w}}_{*}\rangle|\geq 1/4
with probability at least
1-e^{-d^{c_{\ell}}}
. Furthermore,
\hat{\bm{w}}
can be computed with power iteration in
O_{d}(md^{b}\log(d))
runtime.

We prove the sample guarantee of Theorem
9
in Section
F.5
and the runtime guarantee in Section
F.6
. Thus choosing
{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}
such that
\beta_{d,\ell}=\Theta(\|\xi_{d,\ell}\|_{L^{2}})
, the algorithm (

TU

-

Alg

) achieves optimal sample complexity

\mathsf{m}\asymp\frac{d^{\ell/2}}{\|\xi_{d,\ell}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}},

for all
1\leq a<b
. Choosing
a<b
with
a+b=\ell
with smallest runtime, we obtain the following learning guarantees:

\displaystyle\text{$\ell$ even:}

\displaystyle\mathsf{m}\asymp\frac{d^{\ell/2}}{\|\xi_{d,\ell}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}\;\;\text{and}\;\;{\mathsf{T}}\asymp\frac{d^{\ell+1}}{\|\xi_{d,\ell}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}\log(d)\;\;\;\text{by taking $a=\ell/2-1$ and $b=\ell/2+1$},

\displaystyle\text{$\ell$ odd:}

\displaystyle\mathsf{m}\asymp\frac{d^{\ell/2}}{\|\xi_{d,\ell}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}\;\;\text{and}\;\;{\mathsf{T}}\asymp\frac{d^{\ell+1/2}}{\|\xi_{d,\ell}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}\log(d)\;\;\;\text{by taking $a=\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor$ and $b=\lceil\ell/2\rceil$}.

F.2
Notations

Below, we introduce some notations from tensor calculus that will be useful throughout our proofs. Let
{\bm{A}},{\bm{B}}\in({\mathbb{R}}^{d})^{\otimes\ell}
be two
\ell
-tensors. We define the inner-product

\langle{\bm{A}},{\bm{B}}\rangle=\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell}\in[d]}{\bm{A}}_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell}}{\bm{B}}_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell}}.

(77)

In particular, the Frobenius norm of the tensor is
\|{\bm{A}}\|_{F}=\langle{\bm{A}},{\bm{A}}\rangle^{1/2}
.
For
{\bm{A}}\in({\mathbb{R}}^{d})^{\otimes\ell}
and
{\bm{B}}\in({\mathbb{R}}^{d})^{\otimes k}
with
k\leq\ell
, we define the contraction
{\bm{A}}[{\bm{B}}]\in({\mathbb{R}}^{d})^{\otimes(\ell-k)}
to be the
(\ell-k)
-tensor with entries given by

{\bm{A}}[{\bm{B}}]_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell-k}}:=\sum_{i_{\ell-k+1},\ldots,i_{\ell}\in[d]}{\bm{A}}_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell}}{\bm{B}}_{i_{\ell-k+1},\ldots,i_{\ell}}.

(78)

In particular, if
k=\ell
, we have
{\bm{A}}[{\bm{B}}]={\bm{B}}[{\bm{A}}]=\langle{\bm{A}},{\bm{B}}\rangle
. We further introduce partial contraction
{\bm{A}}\otimes_{r}{\bm{B}}
of
{\bm{A}}\in({\mathbb{R}}^{d})^{\otimes\ell}
and
{\bm{B}}\in({\mathbb{R}}^{d})^{\otimes k}
, with
r\leq\min(\ell,k)
, given by

({\bm{A}}\otimes_{r}{\bm{B}})_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell-r},j_{r+1},\ldots,j_{k}}=\sum_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{r}\in[d]}{\bm{A}}_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell-r},s_{1},\ldots,s_{r}}{\bm{B}}_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{r},j_{r+1},\ldots,j_{k}}.

(79)

Given a permutation
\pi\in\mathfrak{S}_{\ell}
and
{\bm{A}}\in({\mathbb{R}}^{d})^{\otimes\ell}
, we define
\pi({\bm{A}})
to be the tensor obtained by permuting the coordinates of
{\bm{A}}
with permutation
\pi
, that is

\pi({\bm{A}})_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell}}={\bm{A}}_{i_{\pi(1)},\ldots,i_{\pi(\ell)}}.

(80)

We define the symmetrization operator
\mathrm{Sym}:({\mathbb{R}}^{d})^{\otimes\ell}\to({\mathbb{R}}^{d})^{\otimes\ell}
such that for each tensor
{\bm{A}}\in({\mathbb{R}}^{d})^{\otimes\ell}
, it outputs its symmetrized version

\mathrm{Sym}({\bm{A}})=\frac{1}{\ell!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathfrak{S}_{\ell}}\pi({\bm{A}}).

(81)

We denote
\mathrm{Sym}((\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\otimes\ell})
the image of this operator, that is, the space of symmetric
\ell
-tensors.

We denote the unfolded matrix of the tensor
{\bm{T}}\in({\mathbb{R}}^{d})^{\otimes\ell}
as
\textbf{Mat}_{q,\ell-q}({\bm{T}})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d^{q}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d^{\ell-q}}
with entries given by

(\textbf{Mat}_{q,\ell-q}({\bm{T}}))_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{q}),(j_{1},\ldots,j_{\ell-q})}={\bm{T}}_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell},j_{1},\ldots,j_{\ell}},

where we identify
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{q})
with
1+\sum_{k=1}^{q}(i_{k}-1)d^{k-1},
and

(j_{1},\ldots,j_{\ell-q})
with
1+\sum_{k=1}^{\ell-q}(j_{k}-1)d^{k-1}.
For clarity, we will drop the dependency on
q
in the proofs, since the unfolding parameter will be made clear. Similarly, with a slight overloading, we denote
\textbf{Mat}_{q,\ell-q}:\mathbb{R}^{d^{\ell}}\to\mathbb{R}^{d^{q}\times d^{\ell-q}}
a folding operation that takes a vector
{\bm{u}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{\ell}}
and associate the matrix

(\textbf{Mat}_{q,\ell-q}({\bm{u}}))_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{q}),(j_{1},\ldots,j_{\ell-q})}=u_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{q},j_{1},\ldots,j_{\ell-q}},

where we identify the index
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell})
with
1+\sum_{k=1}^{\ell}(i_{k}-1)d^{k-1}
.

Throughout the proofs, we denote
C
an absolute constant and
C_{\ell}
a constant that only depends on
\ell
. In particular, the value of these constants are allowed to change from line to line.

F.3
Harmonic tensors and their properties

We start by defining harmonic tensors and present some basic properties about them.

Definition 2

(Harmonic Tensors)
.

For every
\ell>0,
we define
{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}:\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\to\mathrm{Sym}(({\mathbb{R}}^{d})^{\otimes\ell})
the unique symmetric tensor such that for all
{\bm{z}},{\bm{w}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we have

Q_{\ell}^{(d)}(\langle{\bm{z}},{\bm{w}}\rangle)=\langle{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}),{\bm{w}}^{\otimes\ell}\rangle,

(82)

where
Q_{\ell}^{(d)}
is the degree-
\ell
(normalized) Gegenbauer polynomial as defined in Appendix
B
.

In words, harmonic tensors can be seen as the projection of
{\bm{z}}^{\otimes\ell}
into the space of traceless symmetric tensors. Note that the uniqueness of (
82
) follows simply by stating that
\langle{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})-{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}),{\bm{w}}^{\otimes\ell}\rangle
is a degree-
\ell
polynomial in
{\bm{w}}
that is identically zero, and therefore
{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})={\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})
, where we use that
{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})
is assumed to be symmetric.

Further note that these tensors are equivariant with respect to rotations: let
{\bm{O}}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{d}
, then
{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{O}}{\bm{z}})={\bm{O}}^{\otimes\ell}[{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})]
, where the contraction is along one coordinate for each
{\bm{O}}
, that is

{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{O}}{\bm{z}})_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell}}=\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{\ell}\in[d]}\left(\prod_{s\in[\ell]}O_{i_{s}j_{s}}\right){\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{\ell}}.

(This follows simply by noting that
Q_{k}(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{O}}{\bm{z}}\rangle)=Q_{k}(\langle{\bm{O}}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}\rangle)
.)

Recall the zonal property of Gegenbauer polynomials:

Lemma 12

(Zonal property of Gegenbauer polynomials
[
Dai13
]
)
.

Let
f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
. Consider the projection
{\mathsf{P}}_{V_{d,\ell}}
of
f
onto the subspace
V_{d,\ell}
of degree-
\ell
spherical harmonics. This projection can be written as

{\mathsf{P}}_{V_{d,\ell}}f({\bm{x}})=\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{z}}\sim\tau_{d}}[f({\bm{z}})Q_{\ell}^{(d)}(\langle{\bm{z}},{\bm{x}}\rangle)].

(83)

The following lemma follows directly from this property:

Lemma 13

(Reproducing property of harmonic tensors)
.

Let
\ell,k\in{\mathbb{N}}
. We have the identity

\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{z}}\sim\tau_{d}}\Big[Q^{(d)}_{\ell}(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}\rangle){\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})\Big]=\frac{\delta_{\ell k}}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{w}}).

(84)

This property will be key to our analysis of our tensor unfolding algorithm. Indeed, recalling the definition
\beta_{d,\ell}=\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{d}}[{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(Y,R)Q_{\ell}(Z)]
, we have

\mathbb{E}[{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y,r){\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})]=\frac{\beta_{d,\ell}}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{w}}_{*}).

(85)

We further list some useful properties of harmonic tensors below. In particular, the last property states that the principal component of
{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{w}}_{*})
is
\Theta_{d}(d^{\ell/2})\cdot{\bm{w}}_{*}^{\otimes\ell}
, that is the principal component of the expectation of our empirical tensor is
\Theta_{d}(1)\cdot{\bm{w}}_{*}^{\otimes\ell}
.

Proposition 5

(Properties of harmonic tensors)
.

Let
\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})
the harmonic tensor from Definition
2
.

(i)

We have the following explicit formula:

{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})=\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor}c_{\ell,j}\;\mathrm{Sym}({\bm{z}}^{\otimes(\ell-2j)}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{d}^{\otimes j}),

(86)

where

c_{\ell,j}=(-1)^{j}2^{\ell-2j}\frac{\ell!}{j!(\ell-2j)!}\frac{(d/2-1)_{\ell-j}}{(d-2)_{\ell}}\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}},

with
(a)_{p}=a(a+1)\cdots(a+p-1)
the (rising) Pochhammer symbol. In particular, we have
c_{\ell,j}=\Theta_{d}(d^{\ell/2-j})
.

(ii)

Conversely, we have the identity

{\bm{z}}^{\otimes\ell}=\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor}b_{\ell,j}\;\mathrm{Sym}({\mathcal{H}}_{\ell-2j}({\bm{z}})\otimes\mathbf{I}_{d}^{\otimes j}),

(87)

where

b_{\ell,j}=2^{-\ell}\frac{\ell!}{j!(\ell-2j)!}\frac{(d/2+\ell-2j-1)(d-2)_{\ell-2j}}{(d/2-1)(d/2)_{\ell-j}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell-2j}}}.

In particular, we have
b_{\ell,j}=\Theta_{d}(d^{-\ell/2})
.

(iii)

The harmonic tensors satisfy the following recurrence relation:

\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell+1}({\bm{z}})=a^{(1)}_{d,\ell}\;\mathrm{Sym}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})\otimes{\bm{z}})-a^{(2)}_{d,\ell}\;\mathrm{Sym}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell-1}({\bm{z}})\otimes\mathbf{I}_{d}),

(88)

where we denoted
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}):={\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})/\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}
and

a^{(1)}_{d,\ell}=\frac{2\ell+d-2}{d-2+\ell},\qquad a^{(2)}_{d,\ell}=\frac{\ell}{d+\ell-2}.

(iv)

The leading principal component of
{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})
satisfies

\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})-c_{\ell,0}{\bm{z}}^{\otimes\ell}\|_{F}=O_{d}(d^{\ell/2-1/2}),

(89)

where we recall that
\|c_{\ell,0}{\bm{z}}^{\otimes\ell}\|_{F}=c_{\ell,0}=\Theta_{d}(d^{\ell/2})
.

Proof.

The identities in parts (i), (ii) and (iii) simply follows from standard identities on Gegenbauer polynomials. To prove part (iv), using identity (
86
), we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})-c_{\ell,0}{\bm{z}}^{\otimes\ell}\|_{F}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor}|c_{\ell,j}|\|\mathrm{Sym}({\bm{z}}^{\otimes(\ell-2j)}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{d}^{\otimes j})\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor}|c_{\ell,j}|\|{\bm{z}}\|_{2}^{\ell-2j}\|\mathbf{I}_{d}\|_{F}^{j}=\sum_{j=1}^{\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor}|c_{\ell,j}|d^{j/2}=\Theta_{d}(d^{\ell/2-1/2}),

where we used that
|c_{\ell,j}|=\Theta_{d}(d^{\ell/2-j})
.
∎

Additionally, it is interesting to introduce the following tensor:

\Sigma_{\ell}^{(2)}=\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}\;\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{z}}\sim\tau_{d}}\Big[{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})\Big]\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\otimes 2\ell}.

(90)

In particular, by the reproducing property, we have for all
{\bm{u}},{\bm{w}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Q_{\ell}(\langle{\bm{u}},{\bm{z}}\rangle)Q_{\ell}(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}\rangle)]=

\displaystyle\Big\langle\mathbb{E}[{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})],{\bm{w}}^{\otimes\ell}\otimes{\bm{u}}^{\otimes\ell}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}\langle\Sigma^{(2)}_{\ell},{\bm{w}}^{\otimes\ell}\otimes{\bm{u}}^{\otimes\ell}\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}Q_{\ell}(\langle{\bm{u}},{\bm{w}}\rangle),

and we can write

\langle{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}),{\bm{w}}^{\otimes\ell}\rangle=\langle\Sigma^{(2)}_{\ell},{\bm{z}}^{\otimes\ell}\otimes{\bm{u}}^{\otimes\ell}\rangle.

Note that
\Sigma^{(2)}_{\ell}
is only partially symmetric. Using Proposition
5
.(i), we can decompose this tensor explicitly into

\Sigma^{(2)}_{\ell}=\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor}c_{\ell,j}\cdot\mathrm{Sym}_{A}\left(\mathbf{I}_{d}^{\otimes(\ell-2j)}\otimes(\mathbf{I}_{d}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{d})^{\otimes j}\right),

(91)

where we introduced an alternate symmetrizer
\mathrm{Sym}_{A}
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sym}_{A}\left(\mathbf{I}_{d}^{\otimes(\ell-2j)}\otimes(\mathbf{I}_{d}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{d})^{\otimes j}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{r_{1},\ldots,r_{\ell-2j}\in[d]}\mathrm{Sym}({\bm{e}}_{r_{1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes{\bm{e}}_{r_{\ell-2j}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{d}^{\otimes j})\otimes\mathrm{Sym}({\bm{e}}_{r_{1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes{\bm{e}}_{r_{\ell-2j}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{d}^{\otimes j}),

with
({\bm{e}}_{s})_{s\in[d]}
the canonical basis in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

F.4
Proof of Theorem
8

Proof of Theorem
8
.

Denote
\ell=2p
and introduce the matrices

{\bm{Z}}_{i}={\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y_{i},r_{i}){\bm{H}}({\bm{z}}_{i})\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{p}\times d^{p}},\qquad\text{where}\;\;\;{\bm{H}}({\bm{z}}_{i})=\textbf{Mat}_{p,p}({\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}_{i}))\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{p}\times d^{p}},\qquad

and the centered matrices

\overline{{\bm{Z}}}_{i}={\bm{Z}}_{i}-{\bm{E}},\qquad\text{where}\;\;{\bm{E}}=\mathbb{E}[{\bm{Z}}_{i}]=\mathbb{E}[{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y,r){\bm{H}}({\bm{z}})].

By the reproducing property (
85
) and Proposition
5
.(iv), we have

{\bm{E}}=\beta_{d,\ell}\frac{c_{\ell,0}}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}[{\bm{w}}_{*}^{\otimes p}][{\bm{w}}_{*}^{\otimes p}]^{\mathsf{T}}+\beta_{d,\ell}{\bm{\Delta}}_{E},

(92)

where
\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{E}\|_{\rm op}\leq C_{\ell}d^{-1/2}
and
c_{\ell,0}/\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}=\Theta_{d}(1)
.

We consider the symmetric matrix

\hat{\bm{M}}=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i\in[m]}{\bm{Z}}_{i},

and bound
\|\hat{\bm{M}}-{\bm{E}}\|_{\rm op}
using Lemma
25
. First, note that by applying Lemma
14
with
{\bm{A}}={\bm{u}}\otimes{\bm{v}}
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{*}(\hat{\bm{M}}-{\bm{E}})^{2}=

\displaystyle\sup_{{\bm{u}},{\bm{v}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d^{p}-1}}\mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{u}},(\hat{\bm{M}}-{\bm{E}}){\bm{v}}\rangle^{2}]

(93)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa_{\ell}^{2}}{m}\sup_{{\bm{u}},{\bm{v}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d^{p}-1}}\mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{u}},{\bm{H}}({\bm{z}}_{i}){\bm{v}}\rangle^{2}]\leq C_{\ell}\frac{\kappa_{\ell}^{2}}{m}.

Further, note that

\displaystyle\sigma(\hat{\bm{M}}-{\bm{E}})^{2}=\|\mathbb{E}[(\hat{\bm{M}}-{\bm{E}})(\hat{\bm{M}}-{\bm{E}})^{\mathsf{T}}]\|_{\rm op}\leq d^{p}\sigma_{*}(\hat{\bm{M}}-{\bm{E}})^{2}\leq C_{\ell}\kappa_{\ell}^{2}\frac{d^{p}}{m}.

(94)

Using that
\|{\bm{H}}({\bm{z}}_{i})\|_{F}\leq C_{\ell}d^{\ell/2}
deterministically and combining the above displays into Lemma
25
, we obtain with probability at least
1-\delta
that

\|\hat{\bm{M}}-{\bm{E}}\|_{\rm op}\leq C_{\ell}\kappa_{\ell}\sqrt{\frac{d^{p}}{m}}\left[1+\left(\frac{d^{p}}{m}\log^{2}(d/\delta)\right)^{1/2}\vee 1\right].

where we assumed without loss of generality that
\delta\geq e^{-d}
to avoid carrying additional terms.

From Eq. (
92
) and by Davis-Kahan theorem, the leading eigenvector
{\bm{s}}
of
\hat{\bm{M}}
satisfy

|\langle{\bm{s}},{\bm{w}}_{*}^{\otimes p}\rangle|\geq 1-\eta,

with probability at least
1-\delta
when

m\geq C_{\ell}\frac{\kappa_{\ell}}{\eta^{2}}\frac{d^{\ell/2}}{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}}\left[1+\beta_{d,\ell}\log(d/\delta)\right].

The estimator
\hat{\bm{w}}=\textbf{Vec}({\bm{s}})
is obtained by taking the top eigenvector of

\displaystyle\textbf{Mat}_{1,p-1}({\bm{s}})\textbf{Mat}_{1,p-1}({\bm{s}})^{\mathsf{T}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{w}}_{*}{\bm{w}}_{*}^{\mathsf{T}}+\textbf{Mat}_{1,p-1}({\bm{s}}-{\bm{w}}_{*}^{\otimes p})\textbf{Mat}_{1,p-1}({\bm{s}})^{\mathsf{T}}+\textbf{Mat}_{1,p-1}({\bm{w}}_{*}^{\otimes p})\textbf{Mat}_{1,p-1}({\bm{s}}-{\bm{w}}_{*}^{\otimes p})^{\mathsf{T}},

so that

\|\textbf{Mat}_{1,p-1}({\bm{s}})\textbf{Mat}_{1,p-1}({\bm{s}})^{\mathsf{T}}-{\bm{w}}_{*}{\bm{w}}_{*}^{\mathsf{T}}\|_{\rm op}\leq 2\|{\bm{s}}-{\bm{w}}_{*}^{\otimes p}\|_{F}\leq 2\sqrt{\eta}.

Thus, taking
\eta
constant small enough, we obtain
|\langle\hat{\bm{w}},{\bm{w}}_{*}\rangle|\geq 1/4
by Davis-Kahan theorem.
∎

Lemma 14
.

There exists a constant
C_{\ell}>0
such that for all
{\bm{A}}\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\otimes\ell}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{z}}}\left[\langle{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}),{\bm{A}}\rangle^{2}\right]\leq C_{\ell}\|{\bm{A}}\|_{F}^{2}.

(95)

Proof.

Using the identity for the quadratic tensor (
91
), we decompose

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{z}}}\left[\langle{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}),{\bm{A}}\rangle^{2}\right]=

\displaystyle\Big\langle\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{z}}}[{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})],{\bm{A}}\otimes{\bm{A}}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}\big\langle\Sigma^{(2)}_{\ell},{\bm{A}}\otimes{\bm{A}}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor}c_{\ell,j}\frac{1}{(\ell!)^{2}}\sum_{\pi,\pi^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{S}_{\ell}}\big\langle\pi({\bm{A}})[\mathbf{I}_{d}^{\otimes j}],\pi^{\prime}({\bm{A}})[\mathbf{I}_{d}^{\otimes j}]\big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor}\frac{|c_{\ell,j}|}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}d^{j}\|{\bm{A}}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\ell}\|{\bm{A}}\|_{F}^{2},

where we used that
\|{\bm{A}}[\mathbf{I}_{d}^{\otimes j}]\|_{F}^{2}\leq d^{j}\|{\bm{A}}\|_{F}^{2}
and
|c_{\ell,j}|\leq C_{\ell}d^{\ell/2-j}
.
∎

Remark F.1
.

To relax the boundedness assumption in the above proof, the only changes are in the bound on
\sigma_{*}(\hat{\bm{M}}-{\bm{E}})
and
\|{\bm{Z}}_{i}\|_{\rm op}
. First, note that for all
\eta>1
, we have by Hölder’s inequality

\displaystyle\sigma_{*}(\hat{\bm{M}}-{\bm{E}})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sup_{{\bm{u}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d^{p}-1}}\mathbb{E}[{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y,r)^{2}\langle{\bm{u}},{\bm{H}}({\bm{z}}){\bm{v}}\rangle^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{E}[{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y,r)^{2+\eta}]^{1/(1+\eta/2)}\sup_{{\bm{u}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d^{p}-1}}\mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{u}},{\bm{H}}({\bm{z}}){\bm{v}}\rangle^{2+4/\eta}]^{1/(1+2/\varepsilon)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\|{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}\|_{L^{2+\eta}}^{2}(1+2/\eta)^{\ell}\sup_{{\bm{u}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d^{p}-1}}\mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{u}},{\bm{H}}({\bm{z}}){\bm{v}}\rangle^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\ell}(1+2/\eta)^{\ell}\frac{\|{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}\|_{L^{2+\eta}}^{2}}{m}.

where we used hypercontractivity of degree-
\ell
spherical harmonics. Thus as long as
\|{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}\|_{L^{2+\eta}}^{2}=\Theta_{d}(1)
for some
\eta=\Theta_{d}(1)
, the bound does not change. Furthermore, for all integer
q

\mathbb{P}(\max_{i\in[m]}\|{\bm{Z}}_{i}\|_{\rm op}\geq R)\leq\mathbb{P}(\max_{i\in[m]}|{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y_{i},r_{i})|\geq c_{\ell}Rm/d^{p})\leq\left(C_{\ell}\frac{d^{p}}{Rm}m^{1/q}\mathbb{E}[{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}^{q}]^{1/q}\right)^{q}.

If we assume that
\|{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}\|_{L^{q}}\leq q^{C}
for all
q
, we can set
q=\log(m)
and obtain essentially the same guarantees as above. For the second algorithm (

TU

-

Alg

), we can be less careful and simply set
q=C_{\ell}
and only assume
\|{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}\|_{L^{q}}\leq C_{\ell}
.

F.5
Proof of Theorem
9

Proof of Theorem
9
.

Step 1: Decomposing
\|\hat{\bm{M}}-\mathbb{E}[\hat{\bm{M}}]\|_{\rm op}
.

Recall that we fix
\ell=a+b
with positive integers
a\leq b
. Introduce the matrices

{\bm{Z}}_{i}={\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y_{i},r_{i}){\bm{H}}({\bm{z}}_{i})\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{a}\times d^{b}},\qquad\text{where}\;\;\;{\bm{H}}({\bm{z}}_{i})=\textbf{Mat}_{a,b}({\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}_{i}))\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{a}\times d^{b}},\qquad

and the centered matrices

\overline{{\bm{Z}}}_{i}={\bm{Z}}_{i}-{\bm{E}},\qquad\text{where}\;\;{\bm{E}}=\mathbb{E}[{\bm{Z}}_{i}]=\mathbb{E}[{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y,r){\bm{H}}({\bm{z}})].

Recall that by the reproducing property (
85
) and Proposition
5
.(iv), we have

{\bm{E}}=\beta_{d,\ell}\frac{c_{\ell,0}}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}[{\bm{w}}_{*}^{\otimes a}][{\bm{w}}_{*}^{\otimes b}]^{\mathsf{T}}+\beta_{d,\ell}{\bm{\Delta}}_{E},

(96)

where
\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{E}\|_{\rm op}\leq C_{\ell}d^{-1/2}
and
c_{\ell,0}/\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}=\Theta_{d}(1)
.
For convenience, we consider a slight change of normalization and define

\hat{\bm{M}}=\frac{1}{m(m-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}{\bm{Z}}_{i}{\bm{Z}}_{j}^{\mathsf{T}},

such that

\mathbb{E}[\hat{\bm{M}}]={\bm{E}}{\bm{E}}^{\mathsf{T}}=\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}\frac{c_{\ell,0}^{2}}{n_{d,\ell}}[{\bm{w}}_{*}^{\otimes a}][{\bm{w}}_{*}^{\otimes a}]^{\mathsf{T}}+\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}{\bm{\Delta}}_{0},\qquad\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{0}\|_{\rm op}\leq C_{\ell}d^{-1/2}.

(97)

By a standard decoupling argument (see
[
Ver18
,
dlPMS99
]
Chapter 6.1), there exists an absolute constant
C>0
such that

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\hat{\bm{M}}-{\bm{E}}{\bm{E}}^{\mathsf{T}}\right\|_{\rm op}\geq t\right)\leq C\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\tilde{\bm{M}}-{\bm{E}}{\bm{E}}^{\mathsf{T}}\right\|_{\rm op}\geq t\right),

where we defined

\tilde{\bm{M}}=\frac{1}{m(m-1)}\sum_{i\neq j}{\bm{Z}}_{i}\tilde{\bm{Z}}_{j}^{\mathsf{T}},

with
\tilde{\bm{Z}}_{j}={\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(\tilde{y}_{j},\tilde{r}_{j}){\bm{H}}(\tilde{\bm{z}}_{j})
and
(\tilde{y}_{j},\tilde{r}_{j},\tilde{\bm{z}}_{j})_{j\in[m]}
are iid independent of
(y_{i},r_{i},{\bm{z}}_{i})_{i\in[m]}
. Thus, it is enough to study the concentration of
\tilde{\bm{M}}
:

\tilde{\bm{M}}=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i\in[m]}{\bm{Z}}_{i}{\bm{B}}_{i}^{\mathsf{T}},\qquad\text{where}\;\;{\bm{B}}_{i}=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{j\neq i}\tilde{\bm{Z}}_{j}.

Thus we decompose
\tilde{\bm{M}}-{\bm{E}}{\bm{E}}^{\mathsf{T}}={\bm{\Delta}}_{1}+{\bm{\Delta}}_{2}
where

\displaystyle{\bm{\Delta}}_{1}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i\in[m]}\overline{{\bm{Z}}}_{i}{\bm{B}}_{i}^{\mathsf{T}},

(98)

\displaystyle{\bm{\Delta}}_{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i\in[m]}{\bm{E}}\{{\bm{B}}_{i}-{\bm{E}}\}^{\mathsf{T}}=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i\in[m]}{\bm{E}}(\tilde{\bm{Z}}_{i}-{\bm{E}})^{\mathsf{T}}.

We bound the operator norm of these two matrices below.

Step 2: Bound on
\|{\bm{B}}_{i}\|_{\rm op}
.
For all
i\in[m]
, we have

{\bm{B}}_{i}=\tilde{\bm{S}}+\frac{m}{m-1}{\bm{E}}-\frac{1}{m-1}\tilde{\bm{Z}}_{i},\qquad\text{where}\;\;\tilde{\bm{S}}=\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{i\in[m]}\tilde{\bm{Z}}_{j}-{\bm{E}}.

Note that
\|{\bm{Z}}_{i}\|_{\rm op}\leq\|{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}\|{\bm{H}}(\tilde{\bm{z}}_{i})\|_{F}\leq C_{\ell}\kappa_{\ell}d^{\ell/2}
, so that

\displaystyle\|{\bm{B}}_{i}\|_{\rm op}\leq\|\tilde{\bm{S}}\|_{\rm op}+C_{\ell}+C_{\ell}\kappa_{\ell}\frac{d^{\ell/2}}{m}.

(99)

We use Lemma
25
to bound
\|\tilde{\bm{S}}\|_{\rm op}
. Applying Lemma
14
with
{\bm{A}}={\bm{u}}\otimes{\bm{v}}
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{*}(\tilde{\bm{S}})^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{m}\sup_{{\bm{u}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d^{a}-1},{\bm{v}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d^{b}-1}}\mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{u}},\tilde{\bm{Z}}_{j}{\bm{v}}\rangle^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{m}\kappa_{\ell}^{2}\sup_{{\bm{u}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d^{a}-1},{\bm{v}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d^{b}-1}}\mathbb{E}[\langle{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}),{\bm{u}}\otimes{\bm{v}}\rangle^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\ell}\frac{\kappa_{\ell}^{2}}{m}.

To bound
\sigma(\tilde{\bm{S}})
, we simply use

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\bm{S}}\tilde{\bm{S}}^{\mathsf{T}}]\|_{\rm op}=

\displaystyle\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{d^{a}-1}}\mathbb{E}[{\bm{u}}^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\bm{S}}\tilde{\bm{S}}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{u}}]\leq d^{b}\sup_{{\bm{u}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d^{a}-1},{\bm{v}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d^{b}-1}}\mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{u}},\tilde{\bm{S}}{\bm{v}}\rangle^{2}]=d^{b}\sigma_{*}(\tilde{\bm{S}})^{2},

(100)

so that

\sigma(\tilde{\bm{S}})^{2}\leq\max(d^{a},d^{b})\sigma_{*}(\tilde{\bm{S}})^{2}\leq C_{\ell}\kappa_{\ell}^{2}\frac{d^{b}}{m}.

Combining the above displays into Lemma
25
, we get with probability at least
1-de^{-t}
,

\|\tilde{\bm{S}}\|_{\rm op}\leq C_{\ell}\kappa_{\ell}\left[\frac{d^{b/2}}{m^{1/2}}+\frac{t^{1/2}}{m^{1/2}}+\frac{d^{b/3+\ell/6}t^{2/3}}{m^{2/3}}+\frac{d^{\ell/2}}{m}t\right].

We deduce that with probability at least
1-\delta/3
,

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in[m]}\|{\bm{B}}_{i}\|_{\rm op}\leq C_{\ell}\kappa_{\ell}\sqrt{\frac{d^{b}}{m}}\left[1+\left(\frac{d^{a}\log^{4}(d/\delta)}{m}\right)^{1/2}\vee 1\right],

(101)

where we assumed without loss of generality that
\delta>e^{-d}
to avoid carrying extra terms.

Step 3: Bound on
\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{1}\|_{\rm op}
.
Let’s bound
{\bm{\Delta}}_{1}
conditioned on
(\tilde{y}_{i},\tilde{r}_{i},\tilde{\bm{z}}_{i})_{i\in[m]}
. Using Lemma
14
with
{\bm{A}}={\bm{B}}_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{v}}\otimes{\bm{u}}
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{*}({\bm{\Delta}}_{1})^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m^{2}}\sum_{i\in[m]}\sup_{{\bm{u}},{\bm{v}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d^{a}-1}}\mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{u}},\overline{{\bm{Z}}}_{i}{\bm{B}}_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{v}}\rangle^{2}]

(102)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{m^{2}}\sum_{i\in[m]}\kappa_{\ell}^{2}\sup_{{\bm{u}},{\bm{v}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d^{a}-1}}\mathbb{E}[\langle{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}_{i}),({\bm{B}}_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{v}})\otimes{\bm{u}}\rangle^{2}]\leq C_{\ell}\frac{\kappa_{\ell}^{2}}{m}\sup_{i\in[m]}\|{\bm{B}}_{i}\|_{\rm op}^{2}.

Furthermore, similarly to Eq. (
100
),

\sigma({\bm{\Delta}}_{1})^{2}\leq d^{a}\sigma_{*}({\bm{\Delta}}_{1})^{2}\leq C_{\ell}\kappa_{\ell}^{2}\frac{d^{a}}{m}\sup_{i\in[m]}\|{\bm{B}}_{i}\|_{\rm op}^{2}.

(103)

Next, for all
p\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\overline{{\bm{Z}}}_{i}{\bm{B}}_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}\|_{\rm op}^{2p}]^{1/p}\leq

\displaystyle d^{2a}\sup_{{\bm{u}},{\bm{v}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d^{a}-1}}\mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{u}},\overline{{\bm{Z}}}_{i}{\bm{B}}_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{v}}\rangle^{2p}]^{1/p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle d^{2a}p^{\ell}\sup_{{\bm{u}},{\bm{v}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d^{a}-1}}\mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{u}},\overline{{\bm{Z}}}_{i}{\bm{B}}_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{v}}\rangle^{2}]\leq C_{\ell}d^{2a}p^{\ell}\kappa_{\ell}^{2}\sup_{i\in[m]}\|{\bm{B}}_{i}\|_{\rm op}^{2},

where we used the triangular inequality in the first line and hypercontractivity of degree-
\ell
spherical harmonics on the second line. In particular,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{i\in[m]}\|\overline{{\bm{Z}}}_{i}{\bm{B}}_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}\|_{\rm op}^{2}]^{1/2}\leq\frac{1}{m}\left(\sum_{i\in[m]}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\overline{{\bm{Z}}}_{i}{\bm{B}}_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}\|_{\rm op}^{2p}\right]\right)^{1/2p}\leq C_{\ell}\kappa_{\ell}\frac{d^{a}}{m}m^{1/2p}p^{\ell/2}\sup_{i\in[m]}\|{\bm{B}}_{i}\|_{\rm op}.

Taking
p=\log(m)
, we can set

\bar{R}=C_{\ell}\kappa_{\ell}\frac{d^{a}}{m}\log^{\ell/2}(m)\sup_{i\in[m]}\|{\bm{B}}_{i}\|_{\rm op}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i\in[m]}\frac{1}{m}\|\overline{{\bm{Z}}}_{i}{\bm{B}}_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}\|_{\rm op}\geq R\right)\leq\left(C_{\ell}\kappa_{\ell}\frac{d^{a}}{Rm}m^{1/2p}p^{\ell/2}\sup_{i\in[m]}\|{\bm{B}}_{i}\|_{\rm op}\right)^{2p}=\delta.

Taking
p=C\log(m/\delta)
, we can choose

R=C_{\ell}\kappa_{\ell}\frac{d^{a}}{m}\log^{\ell/2}(m/\delta)\sup_{i\in[m]}\|{\bm{B}}_{i}\|_{\rm op}.

(104)

Combining Eqs. (
102
), (
103
) and (
104
) into Lemma
25
, we obtain with probability at least
1-\delta/3

\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{1}\|_{\rm op}\leq C_{\ell}\kappa_{\ell}\left[\sup_{i\in[m]}\|{\bm{B}}_{i}\|_{\rm op}\right]\sqrt{\frac{d^{a}}{m}}\left[1+\left(\frac{d^{a}\log^{\ell+4}(d/\delta)}{m}\right)^{1/2}\vee 1\right],

(105)

where we assumed without loss of generality that
\delta>e^{-d}
to avoid carrying extra terms.

Step 4: Bound on
\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{2}\|_{\rm op}
.
Following the exact same argument as for
{\bm{\Delta}}_{1}
and recalling that
\|{\bm{E}}\|_{\rm op}\leq C_{\ell}
, we directly get with probability at least
1-\delta/3
,

\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{2}\|_{\rm op}\leq C_{\ell}\kappa_{\ell}\sqrt{\frac{d^{a}}{m}}\left[1+\left(\frac{d^{a}\log^{\ell+4}(d/\delta)}{m}\right)^{1/2}\vee 1\right].

(106)

Step 4: Concluding.
Combining the bounds (
97
), (
101
), (
105
) and (
106
), we obtain with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\hat{\bm{M}}-\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}\frac{c_{\ell,0}^{2}}{n_{d,\ell}}[{\bm{w}}_{*}^{\otimes a}][{\bm{w}}_{*}^{\otimes a}]^{\mathsf{T}}\Big\|_{\rm op}\leq

\displaystyle\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{0}\|_{\rm op}+\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{1}\|_{\rm op}+\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{2}\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\ell}\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}d^{-1/2}+C_{\ell}\kappa_{\ell}^{2}\frac{d^{\ell/2}}{m}\left[1+\left(\frac{d^{a}\log^{\ell+4}(d/\delta)}{m}\right)\vee 1\right],

where we used that
a+b=\ell
. Thus by Davis-Kahan theorem, the leading eigenvector
{\bm{s}}
of
\hat{\bm{M}}
satisfy

|\langle{\bm{s}},{\bm{w}}_{*}^{\otimes a}\rangle|\geq 1-\eta,

with probability at least
1-\delta
when

m\geq C_{\ell}\frac{\kappa_{\ell}^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\frac{d^{\ell/2}}{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}}\left[1+\frac{\beta_{d,\ell}}{d^{\ell/4-a/2}}\log^{\ell/2+2}(d/\delta)\right].

The theorem follows by the same argument as in Section
F.4
.
∎

F.6
Runtime of the tensor unfolding algorithm

The overall runtime of the algorithm depends on the runtime for matrix-vector multiplication of the matrices
{\bm{H}}({\bm{z}})=\textbf{Mat}_{a,b}({\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}))
. We show that the total runtime if
\Theta(\max(d^{a},d^{b}))
, that is, one does not need to compute the
d^{a}\times d^{b}=d^{\ell}
entries of
{\bm{H}}({\bm{z}})
to do matrix-vector multiplication with the (unfolded) harmonic tensor. The total runtime of algorithms (

TU

-

Alg

-

b

) and (

TU

-

Alg

) in Theorems
8
and
9
follows by recalling that the leading eigenvector can be obtained with
\Theta_{d}(\log(d))
iterations of the power method.

Lemma 15
.

For integers
a,b\geq 1
with
\ell=a+b
, there exist
C_{\ell}
that only depends on
\ell
such that matrix-vector multiplication with matrix
{\bm{H}}({\bm{z}})=\textbf{Mat}_{a,b}({\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}))
requires at most
C_{\ell}(d^{a}+d^{b})
elementary operations.

Proof.

Using the identity (
86
) in Proposition
5
, we can decompose

{\bm{H}}({\bm{z}})=\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor}c_{\ell,j}\;\textbf{Mat}_{a,b}\left(\mathrm{Sym}({\bm{z}}^{\otimes(\ell-2j)}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{d}^{\otimes j})\right).

Thus, we can decompose
{\bm{H}}({\bm{z}})
into
C_{\ell}
matrices of the following form (without loss of generality):

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\ldots,i_{s_{1}}\in[d],\\
j_{1},\ldots,j_{s_{2}}\in[d],\\
k_{1},\ldots,k_{u}\in[d]\end{subarray}}\left[{\bm{z}}^{\otimes p_{1}}\otimes\bigotimes_{r\in[s_{1}]}{\bm{e}}_{i_{r}}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\bigotimes_{l\in[u]}{\bm{e}}_{e_{k_{l}}}\right]\left[{\bm{z}}^{\otimes p_{2}}\otimes\bigotimes_{r\in[s_{2}]}{\bm{e}}_{j_{r}}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\bigotimes_{l\in[u]}{\bm{e}}_{e_{k_{l}}}\right]^{\mathsf{T}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{u}\in[d]}\left[\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{s_{1}}\in[d]}{\bm{z}}^{\otimes p_{1}}\otimes\bigotimes_{r\in[s_{1}]}{\bm{e}}_{i_{r}}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\bigotimes_{l\in[u]}{\bm{e}}_{e_{k_{l}}}\right]\left[\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{s_{2}}\in[d]}{\bm{z}}^{\otimes p_{2}}\otimes\bigotimes_{r\in[s_{2}]}{\bm{e}}_{j_{r}}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\bigotimes_{l\in[u]}{\bm{e}}_{e_{k_{l}}}\right]^{\mathsf{T}}

where
p_{1}+p_{2}=\ell-2j
,
s_{1}+s_{2}+u=j
,
p_{1}+2s_{1}+u=a
and
p_{2}+2s_{2}+u=b
. The total number of operations to multiply this matrix by a vector is then given by

O_{d}\left(d^{u}(d^{p_{1}+s_{1}}+d^{p_{2}+s_{2}})\right)=O_{d}\left(d^{u+p_{1}+s_{1}}+d^{u+p_{2}+s_{2}}\right)=O_{d}(d^{a}+d^{b}),

which concludes the proof of this lemma.
∎

F.7
Additional discussions

In this section, we provide an additional discussion on the use of the Harmonic tensor. First, note that all the properties discussed in Section
F.3
can be derived using the following Wick’s formula for spherical measure and tedious calculations:

Lemma 16

(Wick’s formula)
.

Let
{\bm{z}}
be a uniform vector on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. We have

\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{z}}}\left[\prod_{j=1}^{2p}{\bm{z}}_{k_{j}}\right]=\frac{1}{d(d+2)\cdots(d+2p-2)}\sum_{\text{pairings $\pi$}}\;\prod_{(a,b)\in\pi}\delta_{k_{a}k_{b}},

(107)

where the sum runs over all
(2p-1)!!
perfect pairings
\pi
of
\{1,2,\ldots,2k\}
.

We note that for our tensor unfolding algorithm, it is enough to consider a simplified tensor
\mathcal{K}_{\ell}
that only keeps the off-diagonal entries of the harmonic tensor.

Definition 3

(Elementary symmetric tensors)
.

For every
\ell
, we define
\mathcal{K}_{\ell}:\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\to\mathrm{Sym}((\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\otimes\ell})
the tensor obtained from
{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}
by putting
0
in the entries with repeated indices: for all
{\bm{z}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, the tensor
\mathcal{K}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})
has entries

\mathcal{K}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell}}=\begin{cases}{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell}}=c_{\ell,0}z_{i_{1}}z_{i_{2}}\ldots z_{i_{\ell}}&\text{ if $i_{1}\neq i_{2}\neq\ldots\neq i_{\ell}$},\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(108)

For convenience, we will denote
{\mathcal{I}}_{d,j}
the set of all subset of
j
indices in
[d]
with no repetitions.
From the reproducing property of
{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})
, we have similarly

\mathbb{E}[{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y,r)\mathcal{K}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})]=\frac{\beta_{d,\ell}}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}\mathcal{K}_{\ell}({\bm{w}}_{*}).

(109)

Apply a random rotation to all the input vectors
{\bm{z}}_{i}
. Equivalently, this amounts to having
{\bm{w}}_{*}\sim\tau_{d}
. This guarantees that
{\bm{w}}_{*}
is not aligned with any coordinate vector with high probability.

Lemma 17
.

Assume
{\bm{w}}\sim\tau_{d}
. Define
\Delta({\bm{w}})=\mathcal{K}_{\ell}({\bm{w}})/c_{\ell,0}-{\bm{w}}^{\otimes\ell}
. Then there exist universal constants
c,C>0
such that for all
t\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}(\|\Delta({\bm{w}})\|_{F}^{2}\geq\ell^{2}(C+t)/d)\leq 2\exp(-cdt^{1/2}).

(110)

Proof.

Simply use that the non-zero entries in
\Delta({\bm{w}})
have at least one repeated index:

\displaystyle\|\Delta({\bm{w}})\|_{F}^{2}\leq\ell^{2}\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{\ell-1}\in[d]}w_{i_{1}}^{2}\cdots w_{i_{\ell-2}}^{2}\cdot w_{i_{\ell-1}}^{4}\leq\ell^{2}\|{\bm{w}}\|_{4}^{4}.

Then the tail bound (
110
) follows from standard concentration argument.
∎

From this lemma, we deduce that

\textbf{Mat}_{p}(\mathbb{E}[{\mathcal{T}}_{\ell}(y,r)\mathcal{K}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})])=\frac{\beta_{d,\ell}}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}c_{\ell,0}\left([{\bm{w}}_{*}^{\otimes p}][{\bm{w}}_{*}^{\otimes(\ell-p)}]^{\mathsf{T}}+{\bm{\Delta}}\right),

(111)

where with probability at least
1-e^{-cd}
over the random rotation, we have
\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{\rm op}\leq C_{\ell}d^{-1/2}
. Thus, it is enough to consider the tensor
\mathcal{K}_{\ell}
which has slightly simpler properties. For example,

\textbf{Mat}_{\ell}\left(\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{K}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})\otimes\mathcal{K}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})]\right)=\frac{c_{\ell,0}}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}\frac{1}{\ell!}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{\ell}}{\bm{P}}_{\sigma},

where
{\bm{P}}_{\sigma}
is the permutation matrix with non-zero entry at row
i
and column
\sigma(i)
.

Lemma 18
.

For integer
\ell\geq 2
and
p=\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor
, there exist
C_{\ell}
that only depends on
\ell
such that matrix-vector multiplication with matrix
\textbf{Mat}_{p}(\mathcal{K}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}))
requires at most
C_{\ell}d^{\lceil\ell/2\rceil}
elementary operations.

Proof.

Let us use Newton–Girard identities to decompose
\mathcal{K}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})
. Note that
\mathcal{K}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})
corresponds to the
\ell
-th elementary symmetric polynomial (in tensor form):

\mathcal{K}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})=\ell!c_{\ell,0}\sum_{i_{1}<\ldots<i_{\ell}}z_{i_{1}}\ldots z_{i_{\ell}}\mathrm{Sym}({\bm{e}}_{i_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\bm{e}}_{i_{\ell}}).

Denote
\lambda\vdash\ell
a partition of
\ell
with
\lambda=1^{a_{1}}2^{a_{2}}\cdots\ell^{a_{\ell}}
so that
\sum_{j\in[\ell]}a_{j}j=\ell
and
|\lambda|=a_{1}+\ldots+a_{\ell}
. Then there exist coefficients
c_{\lambda}
such that (see Lemma
19
below)

\mathcal{K}_{\ell}({\bm{z}})=\ell!c_{\ell,0}\sum_{\lambda\vdash\ell}(-1)^{\ell-|\lambda|}c_{\lambda}\mathrm{Sym}\left(\prod_{j\in[\ell]}\left(\sum_{k_{j}\in[d]}z_{k}^{j}{\bm{e}}_{k}^{\otimes j}\right)^{\otimes a_{j}}\right).

(112)

Let’s decompose
\textbf{Mat}_{p}(\mathcal{K}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}))
as a sum of matrices associated to each
\lambda
(the partition) and
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{\ell}
(the permutation in the symmetrization operator). The number of such matrices only depends on
\ell
. Let us bound the runtime for doing a matrix-vector multiplication for each of these matrices. For each
j\in[\ell]
and
t\in[a_{j}]
, the tensor
{\bm{e}}_{k}^{\otimes j}
has its indices
e_{j,t}+f_{j,t}=j
split into
e_{j,t}
left indices and
f_{j,t}
right indices. Denote
{\mathcal{E}}
the set of
j\in[\ell],t\in[a_{j}]
with
a_{j}>0
, and the subsets
{\mathcal{E}}_{M}\cup{\mathcal{E}}_{L}\cup{\mathcal{E}}_{R}={\mathcal{E}}
that contains respectively the indices with
\{e_{j,t}>0,f_{j,t}>0\}
,
\{e_{j,t}>0,f_{j,t}=0\}
and
\{e_{j,t}=0,f_{j,t}>0\}
. Then we can write the matrix (up to permutation of the indices)

\displaystyle\sum_{\{k_{j,t}\}_{(j,t)\in{\mathcal{E}}}}\prod_{(j,t)\in{\mathcal{E}}}z_{k_{j,t}}^{j}\left(\bigotimes_{(j,t)\in{\mathcal{E}}}{\bm{e}}_{k_{t,j}}^{\otimes e_{j,t}}\right)\left(\bigotimes_{(j,t)\in{\mathcal{E}}}{\bm{e}}_{k_{t,j}}^{\otimes f_{j,t}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\{k_{j,t}\}_{(j,t)\in{\mathcal{E}}_{M}}}\prod_{(j,t)\in{\mathcal{E}}_{M}}z_{k_{j,t}}^{j}\left(\sum_{\{k_{j,t}\}_{(j,t)\in{\mathcal{E}}_{L}}}\prod_{(j,t)\in{\mathcal{E}}_{L}}z_{k_{j,t}}^{j}\bigotimes_{(j,t)\in{\mathcal{E}}}{\bm{e}}_{k_{t,j}}^{\otimes e_{j,t}}\right)\left(\sum_{\{k_{j,t}\}_{(j,t)\in{\mathcal{E}}_{R}}}\prod_{(j,t)\in{\mathcal{E}}_{R}}z_{k_{j,t}}^{j}\bigotimes_{(j,t)\in{\mathcal{E}}}{\bm{e}}_{k_{t,j}}^{\otimes f_{j,t}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}.

For each
\{k_{j,t}\}_{(j,t)\in{\mathcal{E}}_{M}}
, the left and right vectors take
O_{d}(d^{|{\mathcal{E}}_{L}|})
and
O_{d}(d^{|{\mathcal{E}}_{R}|})
operations to compute. Therefore the total runtime is

O_{d}\left(d^{|{\mathcal{E}}_{M}|}\left(d^{|{\mathcal{E}}_{L}|}+d^{|{\mathcal{E}}_{R}|}\right)\right)=O_{d}\left(d^{|{\mathcal{E}}_{M}|+|{\mathcal{E}}_{L}|}+d^{|{\mathcal{E}}_{M}|+|{\mathcal{E}}_{R}|}\right)=O_{d}\left(d^{p}+d^{\ell-p}\right)=O_{d}\left(d^{\lceil\ell/2\rceil}\right),

where we used that
|{\mathcal{E}}_{M}+{\mathcal{E}}_{L}|\leq p
the number of indices in the left side of the matrix, and
|{\mathcal{E}}_{M}|+|{\mathcal{E}}_{R}|\leq\ell-p
the number of indices on the right side of the matrix.
∎

Lemma 19

(Newton-Girard identities)
.

Denote for each integers
\ell,k\geq 1
,

Q_{\ell}({\bm{z}})=\sum_{1\leq i_{1}<\ldots<i_{\ell}\leq d}z_{i_{1}}\cdots z_{i_{\ell}},\qquad P_{k}({\bm{z}})=\sum_{i\in[d]}z_{i}^{k},

the elementary symmetric and power-sum symmetric polynomials respectively. We have

Q_{\ell}({\bm{z}})=\sum_{\lambda\vdash\ell}(-1)^{\ell-|\lambda|}c_{\lambda}P_{1}({\bm{z}})^{a_{1}}P_{2}({\bm{z}})^{a_{2}}\cdots P_{\ell}({\bm{z}})^{a_{\ell}},

(113)

where
\lambda=1^{a_{1}}2^{a_{2}}\cdots\ell^{a_{\ell}}
and

c_{\lambda}=\frac{1}{a_{1}!a_{2}!\cdots a_{\ell}!1^{a_{1}}2^{a_{2}}\cdots\ell^{a_{\ell}}}.

Using Eq. (
113
), we have the following polynomial identity: for all
{\bm{u}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\langle\mathcal{K}_{\ell}({\bm{z}}),{\bm{u}}^{\otimes\ell}\rangle=\ell!c_{\ell,0}Q_{\ell}({\bm{z}}\odot{\bm{u}})=\ell!c_{\ell,0}\sum_{\lambda\vdash\ell}(-1)^{\ell-|\lambda|}c_{\lambda}P_{1}({\bm{z}}\odot{\bm{u}})^{a_{1}}\cdots P_{\ell}({\bm{z}}\odot{\bm{u}})^{a_{\ell}}.

Matching the coefficients in these polynomials in
{\bm{u}}
yields the identity (
112
).

Appendix G
Proofs for Gaussian SIMs

In this, we shall prove the results from
Section

4
. We first start by showing the rates on
L^{2}
norm of coefficients
\xi_{d,\ell}
for a Gaussian SIM of generative exponent
\mathsf{k}_{\star}
(cf.
Lemma

1
).

Proof of
Lemma

1
:

We start by recalling that, from
[
DPVLB24
]
, the generative exponent
\mathsf{k}_{\star}(\rho)
is only defined for
\rho
whose
\nu_{d}
is such that
\nu_{d}\ll\bar{\nu}_{d,0}
, where
\bar{\nu}_{d,0}:=\nu_{d,Y}\otimes\chi_{d}\otimes\tau_{d,1}
is completely decoupled null. In particular,
\lVert\frac{{\rm d}\nu_{d}}{{\rm d}\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}\rVert_{L^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{d,0})}
is bounded by a constant independent of
d
. Let
\{\nu_{d}\}_{d\geq 1}
be the sequence of spherical SIMs associated to the Gaussian SIM
\rho
, i.e.
\nu_{R}=\chi_{d}
and
\nu_{d}(Y\mid Z,R)=\rho(Y\mid Z\cdot R)=\rho(Y\mid X)
. Let
\bar{\nu}_{d,0}=\nu_{Y}\otimes\nu_{R}\otimes\nu_{Z}
.
Let us consider the likelihood ratio decomposition in
L^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{d,0})
identical to the one in
[
DPVLB24
, Lemma D.1]

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\nu_{d}}{{\rm d}\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}(y,r,z)-1

\displaystyle\overset{L^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{d,0})}{=}\sum_{k\geq\mathsf{k}_{\star}}\zeta_{k}(y){\rm He}_{k}(r\cdot z)\,,\quad\zeta_{k}(y)=\mathbb{E}_{(Y,R,Z)\sim\nu_{d}}[{\rm He}_{k}(R\cdot Z)\mid Y=y].

Denote
\lambda_{k}=\lVert\zeta_{k}\rVert_{\nu_{Y}}
, which are completely determined the model
\rho
independent of
d
, and by definition of generative exponent (
2
) we have
\lambda_{\mathsf{k}_{\star}}^{2}>0
. We now use the decomposition of Hermite into Gegenbauer polynomials from
Proposition

2
to rewrite the above as

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\nu_{d}}{{\rm d}\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}(y,r,z)-1

\displaystyle\overset{L^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{d,0})}{=}\sum_{k\geq\mathsf{k}_{\star}}\zeta_{k}(y)\sum_{\ell=0}^{k}\beta_{k,\ell}(r)Q_{\ell}^{(d)}(z)\,=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}Q_{\ell}^{(d)}(z)\sum_{k\geq\mathsf{k}_{\star}}\beta_{k,\ell}(r)\zeta_{k}(y)\,.

(114)

We can now expand the same directly in the Gegenbauer basis

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\nu_{d}}{{\rm d}\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}(y,r,z)

\displaystyle\overset{L^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{d,0})}{=}\bar{\xi}_{d,0}(y,r)+\sum_{\ell\geq 1}\bar{\xi}_{d,\ell}(y,r)Q_{\ell}^{(d)}(z)\,,

(115)

where

\bar{\xi}_{d,0}(y,r)=\frac{{\rm d}\nu_{d,Y,R}}{{\rm d}\nu_{d,Y}\otimes\nu_{d,R}}(y,r)\,\quad\text{ and }\quad\xi_{d,\ell}(y,r)=\mathbb{E}_{(Y,R,Z)\sim\nu_{d}}[Q_{\ell}^{(d)}(Z)\mid Y=y,R=r]

Equating both (
114
) and (
115
), we have the following equalities in
L^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{d,0})

\bar{\xi}_{d,0}(y,r)=\frac{{\rm d}\nu_{d,Y,R}}{{\rm d}\nu_{d,Y}\otimes\nu_{d,R}}(y,r)=1+\sum_{k\geq\mathsf{k}_{\star}}\zeta_{k}(y)\beta_{k,0}(r):=1+\psi(y,r)\quad\text{ for }\quad\psi(y,r)=\sum_{k\geq\mathsf{k}_{\star}}\zeta_{k}(y)\beta_{k,0}(r)\,,

and for
\ell\geq 1

\bar{\xi}_{d,\ell}(y,r)\overset{L^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{d,0})}{=}\bar{\xi}_{d,0}(y,r)\xi_{d,\ell}(y,r)=\sum_{k\geq\mathsf{k}_{\star}}\zeta_{k}(y)\beta_{k,\ell}(r)=\sum_{k\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}\zeta_{k}(y)\beta_{k,\ell}(r)\,,

where
{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}:=\{k\geq\mathsf{k}_{\star}:k\equiv\ell\mod 2\}
. In the last equality, we used the fact that
\beta_{k,\ell}(r)=0
for
\ell\not\equiv k\mod 2
. Our goal is to bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{d}}[\xi_{d,\ell}(y,r)^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\frac{{\rm d}\nu_{d,0}}{{\rm d}\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}(y,r)\xi_{d,\ell}(y,r)^{2}]=\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\bar{\xi}_{d,0}(y,r)\xi_{d,\ell}(y,r)^{2}]

Denoting
\mathcal{K}(y,r):=\bar{\xi}_{d,0}(y,r)\xi_{d,\ell}(y,r)^{2}
, we are interested in calculating
\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\mathcal{K}(y,r)]
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\mathcal{K}(y,r)]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}\left[\mathcal{K}(y,r)(1+\psi(y,r)-\psi(y,r))\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\mathcal{K}(y,r)\bar{\xi}_{d,0}(y,r)]-\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\mathcal{K}(y,r)\psi(y,r)]\,,

(116)

We now have the following claim.

Claim 1
.

We have that for a constant
d
sufficiently large (only in terms of
\mathsf{k}_{\star}
),

|E_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\mathcal{K}(y,r)\psi(y,r)]|\leq\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\mathcal{K}(y,r)]/2\,.

The proof is deferred; for now we use the claim and proceed with the following simplification. It is straightforward to see that, combining Eq. (
116
) with
1
, for
d
sufficiently large (that only depends on
\mathsf{k}_{\star}
), we have

\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}\left[\mathcal{K}(y,r)\bar{\xi}_{d,0}(y,r)\right]\leq\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\mathcal{K}(y,r)]\leq\frac{3}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}\left[\mathcal{K}(y,r)\bar{\xi}_{d,0}(y,r)\right]\,.

(117)

Thus, it suffices to obtain rates on
\mathcal{K}(y,r)\bar{\xi}_{d,0}(y,r)=\bar{\xi}_{d,0}(y,r)^{2}\xi_{d,\ell}(y,r)^{2}=\bar{\xi}_{d,\ell}(y,r)^{2}
by definition. We finally have the following claim which we will show separately to finish the proof of the lemma.

Claim 2
.

For all
\ell\leq\mathsf{k}_{\star}
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\bar{\xi}_{d,\ell}(y,r)^{2}]\asymp d^{-(\mathsf{k}_{\star}-\ell)/2}\text{ for }\ell\equiv\mathsf{k}_{\star}\textnormal{ mod }2\,\,\text{ and }\,\,\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\bar{\xi}_{d,\ell}(y,r)^{2}]\lesssim d^{-(\mathsf{k}_{\star}-\ell+1)/2}\text{ for }\ell\not\equiv\mathsf{k}_{\star}\textnormal{ mod }2\,.

Observe that
2
along with Eq. (
117
) establishes the desired rates on
\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\mathcal{K}(y,r)]=\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{d}}[\xi_{d,\ell}(y,r)^{2}]=\lVert\xi_{d,\ell}\rVert_{L^{2}(\nu_{d})}\,
concluding the proof of the lemma.∎

We now return to the deferred proofs. In order to show
1
, the following bounds on the moments of
\psi(y,r)
will be useful. The idea is to then directly apply
Lemma

24
to conclude that
|\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\mathcal{K}(y,r)\psi(y,r)]|
is vanishing as compared to
\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\mathcal{K}(y,r)]
, which is sufficient to establish
1
.

Claim 3
.

Let
\psi(y,r)=\sum_{k\geq\mathsf{k}_{\star}}\zeta_{k}(y)\beta_{k,0}(r)
, then there exists a universal constant
C>0
such that for
d\geq Cp^{4}
, we have

\lVert\psi\rVert_{L^{p}(\bar{\nu}_{d,0})}\leq C\left(\frac{p}{d^{1/4}}\right)^{\mathsf{k}_{\star}}\,.

Proof.

For the ease of notation we shall denote
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{p}:=\lVert\cdot\rVert_{L^{p}(\bar{\nu}_{d,0})}
and
{\mathcal{I}}=\{k\in{\mathbb{N}}:k\geq\mathsf{k}_{\star},k\equiv 0\text{ mod }2\}
. Recall from
Proposition

2
that
\beta_{k,0}(r)
is a polynomial of degree
k
with only even degree terms when
k
is even and zero otherwise. Using this we have:

\displaystyle\lVert\psi\rVert_{p}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[|\psi(y,r)|^{p}]^{1/p}\leq\sum_{k\in{\mathcal{I}}}\lVert\zeta_{k}\rVert_{p}\lVert\beta_{k,0}\rVert_{p}\leq\sum_{k\in{\mathcal{I}}}\lVert{\rm He}_{k}\rVert_{p}\lVert\beta_{k,0}\rVert_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in{\mathcal{I}}}(p-1)^{k/2}(p-1)^{k/2}\lVert\beta_{k,0}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k\in{\mathcal{I}}}\frac{(p-1)^{k}}{d^{k/4}},

hiding a universal constant. We now note that the summation forms a geometric sequence with ratio
(p-1)^{2}/\sqrt{d}
. Therefore, when
d\geq Cp^{4}
for sufficiently large constant
C
, we have that

\lVert\psi\rVert_{p}\leq C\left(\frac{p}{d^{1/4}}\right)^{\min_{k\in{\mathcal{I}}}k}\leq\,C\left(\frac{p}{d^{1/4}}\right)^{\mathsf{k}_{\star}}\,.

∎

Using the above claim, we are now ready to prove
1
.

Proof of
1
.

Again let
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{p}
denote
\lVert.\rVert_{L^{p}(\bar{\nu}_{d,0})}
. We first evaluate

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{K}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\mathcal{K}(y,r)^{2}]^{1/2}=\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}\left[\frac{{\rm d}\nu_{d}}{{\rm d}\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}(y,r)^{2}\xi_{d,\ell}(y,r)^{4}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}\left[\frac{{\rm d}\nu_{d}}{{\rm d}\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}(y,r)^{2}Q_{\ell}(y,r)^{4}\right]^{1/2}

(Jensen’s inequality)

\displaystyle\lesssim d^{\ell}\left\lVert\frac{{\rm d}\nu_{d}}{{\rm d}\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{d,0})}\lesssim\,\,d^{\ell}\,,

where we used the fact that
\lVert Q_{\ell}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}=\sqrt{d^{\ell}}
and that
\frac{{\rm d}\nu_{d}}{{\rm d}\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}
has
L^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{d,0})
norm bounded by a universal constant by definition of Gaussian SIMs
\rho
. Therefore, we have

\frac{2}{\mathsf{k}_{\star}}\log\left(\frac{\lVert\mathcal{K}\rVert_{2}}{\lVert\mathcal{K}\rVert_{1}}\right)\lesssim\log(d)\,.

Thus for
d
greater than sufficiently large universal constant, we will indeed have
d\geq Cp^{1/4}
for all
p\leq\frac{2}{\mathsf{k}_{\star}}\log(\lVert\mathcal{K}\rVert_{2}/\lVert\mathcal{K}\rVert_{1})
. Using
3
, for
d
greater than sufficiently large universal constant

\lVert\psi\rVert_{p}\leq C\left(\frac{p^{\mathsf{k}_{\star}}}{d^{\mathsf{k}_{\star}/4}}\right)\,.

Invoking
Lemma

24
along with
3
, we obtain that

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\mathcal{K}(y,r)\psi(y,r)]|\lesssim\frac{\lVert\mathcal{K}\rVert_{1}}{d^{\mathsf{k}_{\star}/4}}(2e)^{\mathsf{k}_{\star}}\log^{\mathsf{k}_{\star}}(d)\,.

Note that whenever
\mathsf{k}_{\star}\geq 1
, for
d
greater than some sufficiently large constant (completely determined in terms of
\mathsf{k}_{\star}
), we have

|\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\mathcal{K}(y,r)\psi(y,r)]|\leq\lVert\mathcal{K}\rVert_{1}/2=\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\mathcal{K}(y,r)]/2\,,

as desired. The last equality follows from the fact that
\mathcal{K}(y,r)\geq 0
a.s. under
\bar{\nu}_{d,0}
.
∎

Finally, we prove
2
.

Proof of
2
.

Let us expand
\bar{\xi}_{d,\ell}(y,r)^{2}
under
\bar{\nu}_{d,0}

\bar{\xi}_{d,\ell}(y,r)^{2}=\sum_{k\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}\beta_{k,\ell}(r)^{2}\zeta_{k}^{2}(y)+2\sum_{(k_{2}>k_{1})\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}\zeta_{k_{1}}(y)\zeta_{k_{1}}(y)c_{k_{1},\ell}(r)c_{k_{2},\ell}(r)\,.

\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\bar{\xi}_{d,\ell}(y,r)^{2}]=\sum_{k\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}\mathbb{E}[\beta_{k,\ell}(r)^{2}]\,\lambda_{k}^{2}+2\sum_{(k_{2}>k_{1})\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{k_{1}}(y)\zeta_{k_{1}}(y)]\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{1},\ell}(r)c_{k_{2},\ell}(r)]\,.

(118)

We now use the bound from
Lemma

3
and find the rates for each term in the above.

Case (a)

\ell<\mathsf{k}_{\star}
with
\ell\equiv\mathsf{k}_{\star}\mod 2
: The first term

\sum_{k\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}\mathbb{E}[\beta_{k,\ell}(r)^{2}]\lambda_{k}^{2}\asymp\lambda_{\mathsf{k}_{\star}}^{2}\,d^{-(\mathsf{k}_{\star}-\ell)/2}\,+\sum_{\mathsf{k}_{\star}<k\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}\lambda_{k}^{2}\,\,d^{-(k-\ell)/2}\asymp\lambda_{\mathsf{k}_{\star}}^{2}d^{-(\mathsf{k}_{\star}-\ell)/2}\,,

where in the last step we noticed that the latter term forms a geometric series with decaying ratio whose leading term is of smaller order than the former term. We now show that the sum arising from the cross terms from (
118
) is of smaller order in the absolute value.

\displaystyle|\sum_{(k_{2}>k_{1})\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{k_{1}}(y)\zeta_{k_{1}}(y)]\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{1},\ell}(r)c_{k_{2},\ell}(r)]|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{(k_{2}>k_{1})\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}|\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{k_{1}}(y)\zeta_{k_{1}}(y)]|\cdot|\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{1},\ell}(r)c_{k_{2},\ell}(r)]|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{(k_{2}>k_{1})\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}|\lambda_{k_{1}}\lambda_{k_{2}}|\cdot\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{1},\ell}(r)^{2}]\cdot\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{2},\ell}(r)^{2}]}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{(k_{2}>k_{1})\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}d^{-\left(\frac{k_{1}+k_{2}}{4}-\frac{\ell}{2}\right)}\lesssim\sum_{k_{1}\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}d^{-\left(\frac{k_{1}-\ell}{2}+1\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim d^{-\left(\frac{\mathsf{k}_{\star}-\ell}{2}+1\right)}\,.

Substituting this bound in (
118
), we conclude that for any
\ell<\mathsf{k}_{\star}
with
\ell\equiv\mathsf{k}_{\star}\mod 2
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\bar{\xi}_{d,\ell}(y,r)^{2}]\asymp d^{-(\mathsf{k}_{\star}-\ell)/2}\,.

Case (b)

\ell<\mathsf{k}_{\star}
with
\ell\not\equiv\mathsf{k}_{\star}\mod 2
: We now do similar simplifications.

\sum_{k\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}\mathbb{E}[\beta_{k,\ell}(r)^{2}]\lambda_{k}^{2}\asymp\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}k>\mathsf{k}_{\star}\\
k\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathsf{k}}^{2}\,d^{-(k-\ell)/2}\lesssim d^{-(\mathsf{k}_{\star}+1-\ell)/2}\,.

Bounding the contribution of the cross terms

\displaystyle|\sum_{(k_{2}>k_{1})\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{k_{1}}(y)\zeta_{k_{1}}(y)]\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{1},\ell}(r)c_{k_{2},\ell}(r)]|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{(k_{2}>k_{1})\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}|\lambda_{k_{1}}\lambda_{k_{2}}|\cdot\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{1},\ell}(r)^{2}]\cdot\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{2},\ell}(r)^{2}]}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{(k_{2}>k_{1})\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}d^{-\left(\frac{k_{1}+k_{2}}{4}-\frac{\ell}{2}\right)}\lesssim\sum_{k_{1}\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}d^{-\left(\frac{k_{1}-\ell}{2}+1\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim d^{-\left(\frac{\mathsf{k}_{\star}+1-\ell}{2}+1\right)}\,.

Putting the bounds in (
118
), for any
\ell<\mathsf{k}_{\star}
such that
\ell\not\equiv\mathsf{k}_{\star}\mod 2
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\bar{\xi}_{d,0}(y,r)\xi_{d,\ell}(y,r)^{2}]\lesssim d^{-(\mathsf{k}_{\star}+1-\ell)/2}\,.

∎

We next prove
Lemma

2
which we use to characterize the complexity when one is only allowed to use the directional component
{\bm{z}}
.

Proof of
Lemma

2
:

The proof is very similar to and, in fact, simpler than that of
Lemma

1
. Our goal is to provide rates for the
L^{2}
norm
\xi_{d,\ell}
. However, as
r=1
always as we only observe
(y,{\bm{z}})
, both completely decoupled null and “partially” decoupled null (where
(Y,R)
is decoupled from
Z
) are identical. Therefore, the change-of-measure argument required to used in the proof of
Lemma

1
is no longer needed. The proof then follows from the calculations similar to the one done in the proof of
2
.

For clarity, we will continue to denote the original problem
(y,{\bm{x}})
with
\nu_{d}
, and the new spherical single index model where one only observes
(y,{\bm{z}})
by
\upsilon_{d}
.

Again, we have
\{\nu_{d}\}_{d}
with
\nu_{R}=\chi_{d}
and
\rho(Y\mid X)=\rho(Y\mid Z\cdot R)=\nu_{d}(Y\mid Z,R)
. Let
\bar{\nu}_{d,0}=\nu_{Y}\otimes\nu_{R}\otimes\nu_{Z}
be the completely decoupled null. We will also let
\{\upsilon_{d}\}_{d}
be the sequence of problem associated with
\{\nu_{d}\}_{d}
where we only observe
(y,{\bm{z}})
. We have

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\nu_{d}}{{\rm d}\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}(y,r,z)-1

\displaystyle\overset{L^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{d,0})}{=}\sum_{k\geq\mathsf{k}_{\star}}\zeta_{k}(y){\rm He}_{k}(r\cdot z),\,\text{where }\,\zeta_{k}(y)=\mathbb{E}_{(Y,R,Z)\sim\nu_{d}}[{\rm He}_{k}(R\cdot Z)\mid Y=y]

\displaystyle\overset{L^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{d,0})}{=}\sum_{k\geq\mathsf{k}_{\star}}\zeta_{k}(y)\sum_{\ell=0}^{k}\beta_{k,\ell}(r)Q_{\ell}^{(d)}(z)\,=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}Q_{\ell}^{(d)}(z)\sum_{k\geq\mathsf{k}_{\star}}\beta_{k,\ell}(r)\zeta_{k}(y)\,,

where in the second line, we used the harmonic decomposition of Hermite from
Proposition

2
. We marginalize the radius to explicitly write the likelihood ratio of only
(y,z)
part under
\nu_{d}
and
\bar{\nu}_{d,0}
, is identical to that of
\upsilon_{d}
and
\upsilon_{d,0}=\nu_{Y}\otimes\tau_{d,1}
.

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\upsilon_{d}}{{\rm d}\upsilon_{d,0}}(y,z)-1

\displaystyle\overset{L^{2}(\upsilon_{d,0})}{=}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}Q_{\ell}^{(d)}(z)\sum_{k\geq\mathsf{k}_{\star}}\mathbb{E}[\beta_{k,\ell}(r)]\zeta_{k}(y)\,.

We can also expand the log likelihood ratio of
(y,z)
directly in the Gegenbauer basis

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\upsilon_{d}}{{\rm d}\upsilon_{d,0}}(y,z)-1

\displaystyle\overset{L^{2}(\upsilon_{d,0})}{=}\sum_{\ell\geq 1}\xi_{d,\ell}(y)Q_{\ell}^{(d)}(z)\,,\,\text{where }\,\xi_{d,\ell}(y)=\mathbb{E}_{(Y,Z)\sim\upsilon_{d}}[Q_{\ell}^{(d)}(Z)\mid Y=y].

Equating both, we have for any
\ell\geq 1

\xi_{d,\ell}(y)\overset{L^{2}(\upsilon_{d,0})}{=}\sum_{k\geq\mathsf{k}_{\star}}\zeta_{k}(y)\mathbb{E}[\beta_{k,\ell}(r)]=\sum_{k\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}\zeta_{k}(y)\mathbb{E}[\beta_{k,\ell}(r)]\text{ where }{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}:=\{k\geq\mathsf{k}_{\star}:k\equiv\ell\mod 2\}\,.

Squaring both sides

\xi_{d,\ell}(y)^{2}=\sum_{k\geq{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}\mathbb{E}[\beta_{k,\ell}(r)]^{2}\zeta_{k}^{2}(y)+2\sum_{(k_{2}>k_{1})\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}\zeta_{k_{1}}(y)\zeta_{k_{1}}(y)\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{1},\ell}(r)]\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{2},\ell}(r)]\,.

\lVert\xi_{d,\ell}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\upsilon_{Y})}=\sum_{k\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}\mathbb{E}[\beta_{k,\ell}(r)]^{2}\,\lambda_{k}^{2}+2\sum_{(k_{2}>k_{1})\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{k_{1}}(y)\zeta_{k_{1}}(y)]\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{1},\ell}(r)]\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{2},\ell}(r)]\,.

(119)

We now use the rates on
\mathbb{E}_{r\sim\chi_{d}}[\beta_{k,\ell}(r)]^{2}
from
Lemma

3
to carry out the simplification similar to the one done in the proof of
2
.

Case (a)

\ell<\mathsf{k}_{\star}
with
\ell\equiv\mathsf{k}_{\star}\mod 2
:

\sum_{k\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}\mathbb{E}[\beta_{k,\ell}(r)]^{2}\,\lambda_{k}^{2}\asymp\lambda_{\mathsf{k}_{\star}}^{2}\,d^{-(\mathsf{k}_{\star}-\ell)}\,+\sum_{\mathsf{k}_{\star}<k\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}\lambda_{k}^{2}\,\,d^{-(k-\ell)}\asymp\lambda_{\mathsf{k}_{\star}}d^{-(\mathsf{k}_{\star}-\ell)}\,,

where the step followed by observing that it is a sum of geometric series whose rate is dominated by the first term. We now show the bound on the magnitude of the cross terms

\displaystyle|\sum_{(k_{2}>k_{1})\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{k_{1}}(y)\zeta_{k_{1}}(y)]\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{1},\ell}(r)]\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{2},\ell}(r)]|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{(k_{2}>k_{1})\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}|\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{k_{1}}(y)\zeta_{k_{1}}(y)]|\cdot|\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{1},\ell}(r)]\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{2},\ell}(r)]|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{(k_{2}>k_{1})\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}|\lambda_{k_{1}}\lambda_{k_{2}}|\cdot\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{1},\ell}(r)]^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{2},\ell}(r)]^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{(k_{2}>k_{1})\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}d^{-\left(\frac{k_{1}+k_{2}}{2}-\ell\right)}\lesssim\sum_{k_{1}\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}d^{-\left(k_{1}-\ell+1\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim d^{-\left(\mathsf{k}_{\star}-\ell+1\right)}\,.

Combining these rates with (
119
), we obtain for any
\ell<\mathsf{k}_{\star}
with
\ell\equiv\mathsf{k}_{\star}\mod 2
,

\lVert\xi_{d,\ell}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\upsilon_{Y})}=\mathbb{E}_{\upsilon}[\xi_{d,\ell}(y)^{2}]\asymp d^{-(\mathsf{k}_{\star}-\ell)}\,.

Case (b)

\ell<\mathsf{k}_{\star}
such that
\ell\not\equiv\mathsf{k}_{\star}\mod 2
: We do similar calculation in the other case.

\sum_{k\geq{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}\mathbb{E}[\beta_{k,\ell}(r)]^{2}\lambda_{k}^{2}\asymp\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}k>\mathsf{k}_{\star}\\
k\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathsf{k}}^{2}\,d^{-(k-\ell)}\lesssim d^{-(\mathsf{k}_{\star}-\ell+1)}\,.

Bounding the cross terms

\displaystyle|\sum_{(k_{2}>k_{1})\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{k_{1}}(y)\zeta_{k_{1}}(y)]\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{1},\ell}(r)]\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{2},\ell}(r)]|\leq\sum_{(k_{2}>k_{1})\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}|\lambda_{k_{1}}\lambda_{k_{2}}|\cdot|\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{1},\ell}(r)]\cdot\mathbb{E}[c_{k_{2},\ell}(r)]|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{(k_{2}>k_{1})\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}d^{-\left(\frac{k_{1}+k_{2}}{2}-\ell\right)}\lesssim\sum_{k_{1}\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}d^{-\left(k_{1}-\ell+1\right)}\lesssim d^{-\left(\mathsf{k}_{\star}-\ell+2\right)}\,.

Substituting these bounds in (
119
), for any
\ell<\mathsf{k}_{\star}
such that
\ell\not\equiv\mathsf{k}_{\star}\mod 2
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\nu}_{d,0}}[\xi_{d,\ell}(y,r)^{2}]\lesssim d^{-(\mathsf{k}_{\star}-\ell+1)}\,.

Appendix H
Information-theoretic sample complexity

H.1
Information theoretic lower-bound

Below we derive an information-theoretic lower bound for recovering
{\bm{w}}_{*}
in single-index models under an illustrative assumption. This information-theoretic result completes the low-degree polynomial and SQ lower bounds for the detection problem. Recall that when
\|\xi_{d,1}\|_{L^{2}}=\Theta_{d}(1)
, the
\mathsf{LDP}
lower bounds scales as
\sqrt{d}
: indeed, detection can be achieved with this many samples by taking the test statistics obtained by projecting the likelihood ratio onto samplewise-
(t,1)
polynomials with
t=\omega_{d}(1)
. However, the information-theoretic lower bound for recovery scales as
\Omega(d)
(that is, there is a detection-recovery gap in this model). This is well understood in the Gaussian case (e.g., see
[
MM18
,
MR14
,
DPVLB24
]
) and we provide a short proof for spherical SIMs below for completeness.

We will consider the following illustrative regularity assumptions on the link function.

Assumption 6
.

We assume that for all
s,t\in{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
, we have

\mathsf{KL}(\nu_{d}(\cdot|r,t)||\nu_{d}(\cdot|r,s))\leq dLK(r)(t-s)^{2},

where
L>0
is a constant, and
K\in L^{1}(\mu_{r})
.

Remark H.1
.

In the case of a Gaussian noise i.e
y=f(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{x}}\rangle)+\sigma{\bm{Z}}
, where
{\bm{Z}}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
, we have
\mathsf{KL}\left(\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{w}}}||\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{w}}^{\prime}}\right)=\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\left(f(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{x}}\rangle)-f(\langle{\bm{w}}^{\prime},{\bm{x}}\rangle)\right)^{2}]
which satisfies the
6
.

We now state our minimax lower bound under this assumption.

Theorem 10
.

Let
\nu_{d}\in\mathfrak{L}_{d}
that satisfies
6
and let
\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}}
be the associated family of SIM distributions indexed by
{\bm{w}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. Then, for any estimator
\hat{{\bm{w}}}
based on
\mathsf{m}
observations from
\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}}
, we have:

\inf_{\hat{{\bm{w}}}}\sup_{{\bm{w}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}}}[\|\hat{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}\|^{2}]\geq\frac{(d-1)\log(C)}{8\mathsf{m}LC^{2}},

where
C
is a constant.

Proof of
theorem

10
.

We define the planted distribution denoted by

\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}}^{n}
: given
{\bm{w}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we sample
n
points
(y_{i},{\bm{x}}_{i})\sim_{idd}\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}}
.
We construct the set of hypotheses
\mathcal{H}=\{{\bm{w}}_{1},\ldots,{\bm{w}}_{M}\}
as a
2\delta
-packing of
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
(
\delta
is chosen small enough such that the
2\delta
-packing is not a singleton), and such that
\forall i\neq j,\|{\bm{w}}_{i}-{\bm{w}}_{j}\|\leq{\mathcal{C}}\delta
. Using Fano’s lower bound
[
Wai19
, Chapter 15]
to the problem of estimating
{\bm{w}}
from the observations
(y_{i},{\bm{x}}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
yields

\inf_{\hat{{\bm{w}}}}\sup_{{\bm{w}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}}}[\|\hat{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}\|^{2}]\geq\frac{1}{2}\delta^{2}\left(1-\frac{I({\bm{Z}};J)+\log(2)}{\log(M)}\right),

(120)

where
J
is unformly distributed over
\{1,\ldots,M\}
, and
{\bm{Z}}
is a random variable distributed according to
\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}_{J}}
, where
J
is independent of
{\bm{Z}}
.
By the convexity of the Kullback-Leibler divergence and additivity of the KL divergence, we have

I({\bm{Z}};J)\leq\frac{1}{M^{2}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{M}\mathsf{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}_{i}}^{n},\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}_{j}}^{n})\leq\frac{n}{M^{2}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{M}\mathsf{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}_{i}}||\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}_{j}}).

(121)

Using
6
, we bound the KL divergence between two distributions
\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}_{i}}
and
\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}_{j}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}_{i}},\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}_{j}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{z}},r,{\bm{y}}\sim\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}_{i}}}\left[\log\left(\frac{{\rm d}\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}_{i}}}{{\rm d}\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}_{j}}}(y,r,{\bm{z}})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{z}}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{y}},r\sim\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}_{i}}}\left[\log\left(\frac{{\rm d}\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}_{i}}}{{\rm d}\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}_{j}}}(y,r,{\bm{z}})\right)|{\bm{z}},r\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq L\|{\bm{w}}_{i}-{\bm{w}}_{j}\|^{2}\mathbb{E}_{r}[K(r)]\leq L{\mathcal{C}}\delta^{2},

where
L
is a constant from
6
, and we used that for all
j\neq i,\|{\bm{w}}_{i}-{\bm{w}}_{j}\|\leq{\mathcal{C}}\delta
for some constant
{\mathcal{C}}>0
. We then have

I({\bm{Z}};J)\leq nL{\mathcal{C}}\delta^{2}.

(122)

We bound the cardinality of
M
, using a classical volume argument: let define
{\mathcal{S}}_{\delta}({\bm{x}})=\{{\bm{y}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\colon\langle{\bm{x}},{\bm{y}}\rangle\geq 1-\delta^{2}\}=\{{\bm{y}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\colon\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{y}}\|\leq 2\delta\}
, we then have

{\mathcal{P}}_{\delta}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})\geq\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})\geq\frac{\text{Vol}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\bigcap{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{C}}\delta}(\theta_{1}))}{\text{Vol}({\mathcal{S}}_{\delta})}\geq C{\mathcal{C}}^{d-1},

where
C
is an universal constant, and we denote that
\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
is the covering number and
{\mathcal{P}}_{\delta}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
is the packing number, and we used
\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})\leq{\mathcal{P}}_{\delta}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})

[
Ver18
, Prop 4.2.1]
, and that the homogenity of the volume on the sphere
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
.
With the choice of
{\mathcal{H}}
described above, and plugging this into
eq.

120
and
eq.

121
, we obtain

\inf_{\hat{{\bm{w}}}}\sup_{{\bm{w}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}}}[\|\hat{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}\|^{2}]\geq\frac{\delta^{2}}{2}\left(1-\frac{nL{\mathcal{C}}\delta^{2}+\log(2)}{(d-1)\log({\mathcal{C}})+\log(c)}\right).

(123)

We choose
\delta^{2}=\frac{(d-1)}{2nL{\mathcal{C}}}\leq 1
, and plugging this into the previous inequality
eq.

123
, we obtain the following lower bound for
d
sufficiently large

\inf_{\hat{{\bm{w}}}}\sup_{{\bm{w}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\nu_{d},{\bm{w}}}}[\|\hat{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}\|^{2}]\geq\frac{d-1}{8nL{\mathcal{C}}}.

(124)

∎

H.2
Information theoretic upper-bound
