We complement our information-theoretic lower bound with a sample complexity upper bound. We exhibit an estimator (which is not computable in polynomial time) that achieves strong recovery with
O(d)
samples for general spherically symmetric measure under a mild assumption on the sequence
\{\nu_{d}\}_{d\geq 1}
that there exists
\ell\geq 1
such that
\lVert\xi_{d,\ell}\rVert_{L^{2}}=\Omega_{d}(1)
(cf.
Appendix

A
). The proof is directly adapted from
[
DPVLB24
, Theorem 6.1]
. We focus on proving weak recovery of the ground truth since we can boost it to obtain strong recovery: there exists a (non-polynomial time) algorithm such that for all
\varepsilon>0
, it outputs
\hat{\bm{w}}
with
|\langle\hat{\bm{w}},{\bm{w}}_{*}\rangle|\geq 1-\varepsilon
with probability
1-o(1)
and sample complexity

\mathsf{m}=O(d/\varepsilon).

Proposition 6
.

Under the assumption that there exists
\ell\geq 1
such that
\lVert\xi_{d,\ell}\rVert_{L^{2}}=\Theta_{d}(1)
, there exists an estimator (non polynomially computable) that returns
\hat{{\bm{w}}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
that satisfies
|\langle\hat{{\bm{w}}},{\bm{w}}_{*}\rangle|\geq 1/2
, with probability at least
1-2e^{-d}
with information theoretic sample complexity
\mathsf{m}=O\left(d\right),

hiding constants in
\ell
and
\lVert\xi_{d,\ell}\rVert_{L^{2}}
.

Proof.

For any
\delta>0
, let
\mathcal{N}_{\delta}
be a
\delta
-net of
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, and we can choose
\mathcal{N}_{\delta}
such that
|\mathcal{N}_{\delta}|\leq\left(\frac{3}{\delta}\right)^{d}.
Consider the following
g(y,r,z)=\xi_{d,\ell}(y,r)Q_{\ell}(z)
. For simplicity, let us denote
\beta_{d,\ell}=\lVert\xi_{d,\ell}\rVert_{L^{2}}
Fix a truncation
R>0
, and denote
L_{n}({\bm{w}})
defined as

L_{n}({\bm{w}}):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}_{i}\rangle,y_{i},r_{i})\mathbf{1}_{|g(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}_{i}\rangle,y_{i},r_{i})|\leq R}.

We consider the min-max estimator

\hat{{\bm{w}}}\in\argmin_{\hat{{\bm{w}}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\max_{{\bm{w}}\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}\left|L_{n}({\bm{w}})-\frac{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}Q_{\ell}(\langle{\bm{w}},\hat{{\bm{w}}}\rangle)}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}\right|.

Using
Lemma

24
, we have

\mathbb{E}[g(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}\rangle,y,r)^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[\xi_{d,\ell}(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}\rangle)^{2}Q_{\ell}(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}\rangle^{2})]\leq\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}\log(3/\beta_{d,\ell}^{2})^{k/2}.

Using Bernstein’s lemma, we have for any
{\bm{w}}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, with probability at least
1-2e^{-t}

\left|L_{n}({\bm{w}})-\mathbb{E}[L_{n}({\bm{w}})]\right|\leq\sqrt{\frac{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}\log\left(\frac{3}{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}}\right)^{\ell/2}t}{n}}+\frac{Rt}{n}.

By union bound and setting
t=d\left(\log\left(\frac{3}{\delta}\right)+1\right)
, we then have with probability at least
1-2e^{-d},

\sup_{{\bm{w}}\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}\left|L_{n}({\bm{w}})-\mathbb{E}[L_{n}({\bm{w}})]\right|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}\log\left(\frac{3}{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}}\right)^{k/2}d\log\left(\frac{3}{\delta}\right)}{n}}+\frac{Rd\left(\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\right)}{n}.

(125)

We bound the effect of the truncation

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[L_{n}({\bm{w}})-\mathbb{E}[g(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}\rangle,y,r)]]|

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{E}[g(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}\rangle,y,r)1_{|g(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}\rangle,y,r)|\geq R}]|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[g(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}\rangle,y,r)^{2}]\mathbb{P}(|g(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}\rangle,y,r)|>R)}

\displaystyle\leq\beta_{d,\ell}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}(|g(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}\rangle,y,r)|>R)}\,.

We then have the following control on the moments of
|g(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}\rangle,y,r))|

\mathbb{E}[|g(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}\rangle,y,r)|^{p}]^{1/p}\leq\mathbb{E}[|Q_{\ell}(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}\rangle)|^{p}|\xi_{d,\ell}(y,r)|^{p}]^{1/p}\leq(2p)^{\ell},

by using Jensen inequality and spherical hypercontractivity.
By taking
R\geq(2e)^{\ell}
, and
\delta=R^{1/\ell}/2e

\mathbb{P}(|g(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}\rangle,y,r)|>R)\leq\frac{2p^{\ell p}}{R^{p}}\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\ell}{2e}R^{1/\ell}\right)

Combining the two inequalities gives

\left|\mathbb{E}[L_{n}({\bm{w}})]-\mathbb{E}[g(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}\rangle,y,r)]\right|\leq\beta_{d,\ell}\exp\left(-\frac{\ell}{4e}R^{1/\ell}\right).

Combining the above inequalities, we then have

\displaystyle\max_{{\bm{w}}\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}\left|\frac{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}Q_{\ell}(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{w}}_{*}\rangle)}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}-\frac{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}Q_{\ell}(\langle{\bm{w}},\hat{{\bm{w}}}\rangle)}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{{\bm{w}}\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}\left|L_{n}({\bm{w}})-\frac{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}Q_{\ell}(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{w}}_{*}\rangle)}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}\right|+\max_{{\bm{w}}\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}\left|L_{n}({\bm{w}})-\frac{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}Q_{\ell}(\langle{\bm{w}},\hat{{\bm{w}}}\rangle)}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\max_{{\bm{w}}\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}\left|L_{n}({\bm{w}})-\frac{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}Q_{\ell}(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{w}}_{*}\rangle)}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\max_{{\bm{w}}\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}\left|L_{n}({\bm{w}})-\mathbb{E}\left[g(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{z}}\rangle,y,r)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{{\bm{w}}\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}\left|L_{n}({\bm{w}})-\mathbb{E}\left[L_{n}({\bm{w}})\right]\right|+3^{\ell}\exp\left(-\frac{\ell}{4e}R^{1/\ell}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}\log\left(\frac{3}{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}}\right)^{k/2}t}{n}}+\frac{Rt}{n}+3^{\ell}\exp\left(-\frac{\ell}{4e}R^{1/\ell}\right).

We have the following

\displaystyle\left|\frac{Q_{\ell}(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{w}}_{*}\rangle)-Q_{\ell}(\langle{\bm{w}},\hat{{\bm{w}}}\rangle)}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}\left|\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor}c_{\ell,q}\left(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{w}}_{*}\rangle^{\ell-2q}-\langle{\bm{w}},\hat{{\bm{w}}}\rangle^{\ell-2q}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{\ell,0}}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}\left|\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{w}}_{*}\rangle^{\ell}-\langle{\bm{w}},\hat{{\bm{w}}}\rangle^{\ell}\right|+O(d^{-1}).

We then deduce that

\max_{{\bm{w}}\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}\left|\frac{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}Q_{\ell}(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{w}}_{*}\rangle)}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}-\frac{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}Q_{\ell}(\langle{\bm{w}},\hat{{\bm{w}}}\rangle)}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}\right|=\frac{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}c_{\ell,0}}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}\max_{{\bm{w}}\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}\left|\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{w}}_{*}\rangle^{\ell}-\langle{\bm{w}},\hat{{\bm{w}}}\rangle^{\ell}\right|+O(d^{-1}).

Using
[
DH24
, Lemma 25]
, we then have

\frac{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}c_{\ell,0}}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}\min_{s\in\{\pm 1\}}\|s\hat{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{*}\|\lesssim\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}\left(\max_{{\bm{w}}\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}\left|\frac{Q_{\ell}(\langle{\bm{w}},{\bm{w}}_{*}\rangle)}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}-\frac{Q_{\ell}(\langle{\bm{w}},\hat{{\bm{w}}}\rangle)}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}\right|+\delta+O(d^{-1})\right)

Using this inequality above, and plugging the inequality, we have

\min_{s\in\{\pm 1\}}\|s\hat{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}\log(\frac{3}{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}})^{\ell/2}d\log\left(\frac{3}{\delta}\right)}{n}}+\delta+\frac{Rd\log\left(\frac{3}{\delta}\right)}{n}+3^{\ell}\exp\left(-\frac{\ell}{4e}R^{1/\ell}\right)

Choosing
R=(4e\log(3/\delta))^{\ell}
, it yields

\frac{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}c_{\ell,0}}{\sqrt{n_{d,\ell}}}\min_{s\in\{\pm 1\}}\|s\hat{{\bm{w}}}-{\bm{w}}_{*}\|\lesssim\delta+\sqrt{\frac{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}\log(\frac{3}{\beta_{d,\ell}^{2}})^{\ell/2}d\log\left(\frac{3}{\delta}\right)}{n}}+\frac{Rd\log\left(\frac{3}{\delta}\right)}{n}+3^{\ell}\exp\left(-\frac{\ell}{4e}R^{1/\ell}\right).

Taking
\delta=O(\beta_{d,\ell}^{2})
concludes the proof.
∎

Appendix I
Additional technical lemmas

The uniform distribution
\tau_{d}={\rm Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
and the isotropic Gaussian distribution
{\sf N}(0,\mathbf{I}_{d})
satisfy the following hypercontractivity properties:

Lemma 20

(Spherical Hypercontractivity
[
Bec92
]
)
.

For any
\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
f\in L^{2}(\tau_{d})
which is a degree
\ell
polynomial, for any
p\geq 2
, we have

\lVert f\rVert_{L^{p}(\tau_{d})}\leq(p-1)^{\ell/2}\,\lVert f\rVert_{L^{2}(\tau_{d})}\,.

Lemma 21

(Gaussian Hypercontractivity)
.

For any
\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
f\in L^{2}({\sf N}({\bm{0}},\mathbf{I}_{d}))
which is a degree
\ell
polynomial, for any
p\geq 2
, we have

\lVert f\rVert_{L^{p}}:=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}\sim{\sf N}({\bm{0}},\mathbf{I}_{d})}[|f({\bm{x}})|^{p}]^{1/p}\leq(p-1)^{\ell/2}\,\lVert f\rVert_{L^{2}}\,.

As a corollary of this, we also have the following property for the
\chi_{d}
distribution.

Lemma 22
.

For any even
\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
f\in L^{2}(\chi_{d})
which is a polynomial of degree
\ell
with only even degree terms (i.e.
f(r)=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell/2}a_{i}\,r^{2i}
), for any
p\geq 2
we have

\lVert f\rVert_{L^{p}(\chi_{d})}\leq(p-1)^{\ell/2}\lVert f\rVert_{L^{2}(\chi_{d})}\,.

This lemma follows from
Lemma

21
by noting that
f(r)=f(\lVert{\bm{x}}\rVert_{2})=f(\sqrt{x_{1}^{2}+\dots+x_{d}^{2}})
is a polynomial of
{\bm{x}}
of degree
\ell
, where
{\bm{x}}\sim{\sf N}(0,\mathbf{I}_{d})
. To obtain high probability tail bounds from bounds on the moments, we will often use the above hypercontractivity properties with the following standard tail-bounds:

Lemma 23

(Lemma 24 in
[
DNGL24
]
)
.

Let
\delta\geq 0
and
X
be a mean zero random variable satisfying

\mathbb{E}[|X|^{p}]^{1/p}\leq B\,p^{k/2}\;\text{ for }p=\frac{2\log(1/\delta)}{k}\,,

for some
k
. Then with probability
1-\delta
, we have
|X|\leq B\,p^{k/2}\,.

Similar to
[
DNGL24
]
, we will use the following lemma to bound
\mathbb{E}[XY]
instead of standard Cauchy-Schwarz, when we have a tight bound
\lVert X\rVert_{1}
and all moments
\lVert Y\rVert_{p}
but a very loose bound on
\lVert X\rVert_{2}
.

Lemma 24

(Lemma 23 in
[
DNGL24
]
)
.

Let
X,Y
be random variables with
\lVert Y\rVert_{p}\leq B\,p^{k/2}
. Then

\mathbb{E}[XY]\leq\lVert X\rVert_{1}\cdot B\cdot(2e)^{k/2}\cdot\max\left(1,\frac{2}{k}\log\left(\frac{\lVert X\rVert_{2}}{\lVert X\rVert_{1}}\right)\right)^{k/2}\,.

Lemma 25

(Lemma I.5 in
[
DPVLB24
]
)
.

Let
{\bm{Y}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bm{Z}}_{i}
, where
{\bm{Z}}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times q}
are mean zero independent matrices. Define

\displaystyle\sigma:=\sigma({\bm{Y}})=

\displaystyle\max\left(\|\mathbb{E}[{\bm{Y}}{\bm{Y}}^{\mathsf{T}}]\|_{{\rm op}}^{1/2},\|\mathbb{E}[{\bm{Y}}^{\mathsf{T}}{\bm{Y}}]\|_{{\rm op}}^{1/2}\right),

\displaystyle\sigma_{*}:=\sigma_{*}({\bm{Y}})=

\displaystyle\sup_{{\bm{v}}\in\mathbb{S}^{p-1},{\bm{u}}\in\mathbb{S}^{q-1}}\mathbb{E}[\left(\langle{\bm{v}},{\bm{Y}}{\bm{w}}\rangle\right)^{2}]^{1/2},

\displaystyle\bar{R}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\max_{i\in[n]}\|{\bm{Z}}_{i}\|_{\rm op}^{2}]^{1/2}.

Then for

R\geq\bar{R}^{1/2}\sigma^{1/2}+\sqrt{2}\bar{R},

and
t\geq 0
, denoting
\delta=\mathbb{P}(\max_{i\in[n]}\|{\bm{Z}}_{i}\|\geq R)
, we have with probability at least
1-\delta-de^{-t}
,

\|{\bm{Y}}-\mathbb{E}[{\bm{Y}}]\|_{\rm op}\leq 2\sigma+\sigma_{*}t^{1/2}+R^{1/3}\sigma^{2/3}t^{2/3}+Rt.

Lemma 26
.

Let
\{Z_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
be a sequence of independent random variables with polynomial tails, i.e. there exists
B,k
such that
\mathbb{E}[|Z_{i}|^{p}]^{1/p}\leq Bp^{k/2}
. Define
R=\max_{i\in[n]}Z_{i}
. Then for any
p\leq\log n/k
, we have

\mathbb{E}[|R|^{p}]^{1/p}\leq B\log^{k/2}(n)
and for any
\delta\geq 0
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,
R\leq B\log^{k/2}(n/\delta)
.

Lemma 27

(Lemma I.3 from
[
DPVLB24
]
)
.

Let
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
be independent mean zero random variables such that for all
p\geq 2
, we have
\lVert X_{i}\rVert_{p}\leq Bp^{k/2}
for some
k
and let
\sigma^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]
. Let
Y=\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}
. Then with pribability at least
1-\delta
,

|Y|\lesssim_{k}\,\sigma\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}+B\log(1/\delta)\log(n/\delta)^{k/2}\,.

Lemma 28

(
[
T+15
]
)
.

Let
{\bm{X}}_{k}
be i.i.d random matrices of dimensions
d_{1}\times d_{2}.
Assume that each matrix is bounded by

\forall k,\|{\bm{X}}_{k}-\mathbb{E}[{\bm{X}}_{k}]\|_{{\rm op}}\leq L.

Consider
v({\bm{Z}})=\max\{\|\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[({\bm{X}}_{k}-\mathbb{E}[{\bm{X}}_{k}])({\bm{X}}_{k}-\mathbb{E}[{\bm{X}}_{k}])^{\mathsf{T}}]\|,\|\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[({\bm{X}}_{k}-\mathbb{E}[{\bm{X}}_{k}])^{\mathsf{T}}({\bm{X}}_{k}-\mathbb{E}[{\bm{X}}_{k}])]\|\},
then with probability at least
1-\delta,

\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{n}({\bm{X}}_{k}-\mathbb{E}[{\bm{X}}_{k}])\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq\frac{L}{3}\log\left(\frac{d_{1}+d_{2}}{\delta}\right)+\sqrt{4v\log\left(\frac{d_{1}+d_{2}}{\delta}\right)}.
